1. **Create a new `run_command` tool** that lets the agent execute any slash command by calling its own local control API (`POST http://127.0.0.1:{port}/sessions/_any/message`). This replaces the special-purpose `switch_model` and `smart_model` tools.

```
1→"""
     2→switch_model tool — lets the agent discover available models/providers
     3→and switch to a different one mid-conversation.
     4→
     5→Two actions:
     6→  - list: show current model, original model, and available providers+models
     7→  - switch: change to a different provider/model at runtime

    14→

    18→def switch_model_list(
    19→    current_provider: str,
    20→    current_model: str,
    21→    original: dict,
    22→) -> str:
    23→    """Return JSON describing current state and available providers/models."""
    24→    from hermes_cli.models import list_available_providers, curated_models_for_provider
    25→
    26→    available = []
    27→    for prov in list_available_providers():
    28→        models = curated_models_for_provider(prov["id"])
    29→        available.append({
    30→            "provider": prov["id"],
    31→            "label": prov["label"],
    32→            "authenticated": prov["authenticated"],
    33→            "models": [m_id for m_id, _ in models],
    34→        })
    35→
    36→    return json.dumps({
    37→        "current": {"provider": current_provider, "model": current_model},
    38→        "original": original,
    39→        "available_providers": available,
    40→    }, ensure_ascii=False)
    41→
    42→
    43→def switch_model_switch(
    44→    provider: str,
    45→    model: str,
    46→    reason: str,
    47→    agent,  # AIAgent — imported at call site to avoid circular import
    48→) -> str:
    49→    """Ask the agent to switch its model/provider in-place.
    50→
    51→    Delegates to agent._switch_model() which handles client construction.
    52→    Returns JSON the agent sees as its tool result.
    53→    """
    54→    if not provider or not model:
    55→        return json.dumps({
    56→            "success": False,

    60→
    61→    if reason:

    63→
    64→    result = agent._switch_model(provider, model)
```

```
1→"""
     2→smart_model — PoC tool that automatically picks and switches to the best model
     3→for a given task type.
     4→
     5→The agent calls this with a task description, and the tool picks an appropriate
     6→model (e.g., fast/cheap for simple tasks, powerful for complex ones) and switches.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→import json
    10→import logging
    11→
    12→from tools.registry import registry
    13→
    14→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    15→
    16→# Simple task-to-model mapping — customize as needed
    17→MODEL_PRESETS = {
    18→    "fast": {
    19→        "provider": "openrouter",
    20→        "model": "google/gemini-2.5-flash",
    21→        "description": "Fast and cheap — good for simple lookups, summaries, formatting",
    22→    },
    23→    "balanced": {
    24→        "provider": "openrouter",
    25→        "model": "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4",
    26→        "description": "Good balance of speed and capability",
    27→    },
    28→    "powerful": {
    29→        "provider": "openrouter",
    30→        "model": "anthropic/claude-opus-4",
    31→        "description": "Most capable — complex reasoning, coding, analysis",
    32→    },
    33→    "code": {
    34→        "provider": "openrouter",
    35→        "model": "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4",
    36→        "description": "Optimized for code generation and debugging",
    37→    },
    38→}
    39→
    40→
    41→def smart_model_handle(agent, preset: str, custom_provider: str = "", custom_model: str = "") -> str:
    42→    """Pick a model based on preset or custom provider/model, then switch."""
    43→
    44→    old_model = agent.model
    45→    old_provider = agent.provider
    46→
    47→    # Use custom if provided, otherwise look up preset
    48→    if custom_provider and custom_model:
    49→        provider = custom_provider
    50→        model = custom_model
    51→    elif preset in MODEL_PRESETS:
    52→        provider = MODEL_PRESETS[preset]["provider"]
    53→        model = MODEL_PRESETS[preset]["model"]
    54→    else:
    55→        return json.dumps({
    56→            "success": False,
    57→            "error": f"Unknown preset '{preset}'. Available: {list(MODEL_PRESETS.keys())}",
    58→            "current": {"provider": old_provider, "model": old_model},
    59→        })
    60→
    61→    # Already on this model? No-op.
    62→    if model == old_model and provider == old_provider:
    63→        return json.dumps({
    64→            "success": True,
    65→            "message": f"Already using {model}",
    66→            "current": {"provider": provider, "model": model},
    67→        })
    68→
    69→    # Delegate to agent's built-in switch
    70→    result = agent._switch_model(provider, model)
    71→    if result.get("success"):
    72→        result["preset"] = preset if preset in MODEL_PRESETS else "custom"
    73→    return json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False)
    74→
    75→
    76→# ── Schema ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    77→
    78→SMART_MODEL_SCHEMA = {
    79→    "name": "smart_model",
    80→    "description": (
    81→        "Switch to a model preset suited for the current task. "
    82→        "Presets: 'fast' (cheap/quick), 'balanced' (general), 'powerful' (complex reasoning), "
    83→        "'code' (programming). Or provide custom provider+model."
    84→    ),
    85→    "parameters": {
    86→        "type": "object",
    87→        "properties": {
    88→            "preset": {
    89→                "type": "string",
    90→                "enum": ["fast", "balanced", "powerful", "code", "custom"],
    91→                "description": "Model preset to use, or 'custom' with provider+model.",
    92→            },
    93→            "provider": {
    94→                "type": "string",
    95→                "description": "Custom provider (only with preset='custom'). E.g. 'openrouter'.",
    96→            },
    97→            "model": {
    98→                "type": "string",
    99→                "description": "Custom model ID (only with preset='custom'). E.g. 'openai/gpt-4o'.",
   100→            },
   101→        },
   102→        "required": ["preset"],
   103→    },
   104→}
   105→
   106→
   107→def _check_smart_model() -> bool:
   108→    return True
   109→
   110→
   111→registry.register(
   112→    name="smart_model",
   113→    toolset="smart_model",
   114→    schema=SMART_MODEL_SCHEMA,
   115→    handler=lambda args, **kw: json.dumps({
   116→        "error": "smart_model must be handled by the agent loop"
   117→    }),
   118→    check_fn=_check_smart_model,
   119→)
   120→
```

```
1→"""
     2→Local-only HTTP control API for the Hermes gateway.
     3→
     4→Runs alongside the gateway as a background aiohttp server on 127.0.0.1.
     5→Provides programmatic control over live agent sessions — session listing,
     6→message injection, etc.
     7→
     8→External tools (e.g., desloppify) can call these endpoints to influence
     9→a running Hermes agent without needing direct process access.
    10→
    11→All mutations go through POST /sessions/{key}/message — inject any text
    12→(including /commands) as if the user typed it.
    13→
    14→Default port: 47823 (override with HERMES_CONTROL_PORT env var).
    15→"""
    16→
    17→import json
    18→import logging
    19→import os
    20→
    21→from aiohttp import web
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→DEFAULT_PORT = 47823
    26→
    27→# Commands available via /message injection.  This list is informational —
    28→# the message endpoint accepts any text, but GET /commands exposes these
    29→# so external tools know what's available.
    30→AVAILABLE_COMMANDS = [
    31→    {"command": "/reset", "description": "Reset conversation history"},
    32→    {"command": "/new", "description": "Start a new conversation"},
    33→    {"command": "/stop", "description": "Stop the running agent"},
    34→    {"command": "/compact", "description": "Compress conversation context"},
    35→    {"command": "/compress", "description": "Compress conversation context"},
    36→    {"command": "/status", "description": "Show session info"},
    37→    {"command": "/model <provider:model>", "description": "Switch model"},
    38→    {"command": "/personality <name>", "description": "Switch personality"},
    39→    {"command": "/autoreply <instructions>", "description": "Enable auto-reply loop"},
    40→    {"command": "/autoreply off", "description": "Disable auto-reply loop"},
    41→    {"command": "/reasoning <level>", "description": "Set reasoning effort"},
    42→    {"command": "/rollback [number]", "description": "List or restore checkpoints"},
    43→    {"command": "/background <prompt>", "description": "Run prompt in background session"},
    44→    {"command": "/undo", "description": "Undo last assistant response"},
    45→    {"command": "/retry", "description": "Retry last message"},
    46→    {"command": "/usage", "description": "Show token usage"},
    47→    {"command": "/help", "description": "List commands"},
    48→]
    49→
    50→
    51→def _get_port() -> int:
    52→    return int(os.getenv("HERMES_CONTROL_PORT", str(DEFAULT_PORT)))
    53→
    54→
    55→def _json_response(data: dict, status: int = 200) -> web.Response:
    56→    return web.Response(
    57→        text=json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False),
    58→        content_type="application/json",
    59→        status=status,
    60→    )
    61→
    62→
    63→class ControlAPI:
    64→    """Lightweight control API that holds a reference to the GatewayRunner."""
    65→
    66→    def __init__(self, gateway_runner):
    67→        self.runner = gateway_runner
    68→        self.app = web.Application()
    69→        self._setup_routes()
    70→        self._site = None
    71→
    72→    def _setup_routes(self):
    73→        self.app.router.add_get("/health", self.health)
    74→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions", self.list_sessions)
    75→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions/{key}", self.get_session)
    76→        self.app.router.add_get("/commands", self.list_commands)
    77→        self.app.router.add_post("/sessions/{key}/message", self.send_message)
    78→
    79→    # ── Helpers ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    80→
    81→    def _resolve_agent(self, key: str):
    82→        """Look up a running agent by session key.  '_any' returns the first.
    83→        Returns (resolved_key, agent) or (key, None) if not found."""
    84→        if key == "_any":
    85→            if not self.runner._running_agents:
    86→                return key, None
    87→            key = next(iter(self.runner._running_agents))
    88→        return key, self.runner._running_agents.get(key)
    89→
    90→    # ── Endpoints ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    91→
    92→    async def health(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    93→        return _json_response({"status": "ok"})
    94→
    95→    async def list_sessions(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    96→        """List all sessions, marking which have a live (running) agent."""
    97→        sessions = []
    98→        for key, agent in self.runner._running_agents.items():
    99→            entry = {
   100→                "session_key": key,
   101→                "status": "running",
   102→                "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
   103→                "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
   104→                **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   105→            }
   106→            sessions.append(entry)
   107→        return _json_response({"sessions": sessions})
   108→
   109→    async def get_session(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   110→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   111→        key, agent = self._resolve_agent(key)
   112→        if not agent:
   113→            return _json_response(
   114→                {"error": f"No running agent for '{key}'"},
   115→                status=404,
   116→            )
   117→        return _json_response({
   118→            "session_key": key,
   119→            "status": "running",
   120→            "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
   121→            "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
   122→            **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   123→        })
   124→
   125→    async def list_commands(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   126→        """List available slash commands that can be sent via /message."""
   127→        return _json_response({"commands": AVAILABLE_COMMANDS})
   128→
   129→    async def send_message(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   130→        """Inject a message (or /command) into a session.
   131→
   132→        POST body: {"text": "...", "mode": "interrupt"|"queue"}
   133→
   134→        Modes:
   135→        - interrupt (default): cancel current agent run, process this message
   136→        - queue: let current run finish, then process this message next
   137→        """
   138→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   139→
   140→        try:
   141→            body = await request.json()
   142→        except Exception:
   143→            return _json_response({"error": "Invalid JSON body"}, status=400)
   144→
   145→        text = body.get("text", "").strip()
   146→        if not text:
   147→            return _json_response({"error": "'text' is required"}, status=400)
   148→
   149→        mode = body.get("mode", "interrupt")
   150→        if mode not in ("interrupt", "queue"):
   151→            return _json_response(
   152→                {"error": f"Invalid mode '{mode}', must be 'interrupt' or 'queue'"},
   153→                status=400,
   154→            )
   155→
   156→        logger.info(
   157→            "Control API: send_message session=%s mode=%s text=%s",
   158→            key, mode, text[:80],
   159→        )
   160→
   161→        try:
   162→            result = self.runner.inject_message(key, text, interrupt=(mode != "queue"))
   163→            # await if coroutine (GatewayRunner is async, CLI shim is sync)
   164→            if hasattr(result, "__await__"):
   165→                await result
   166→        except LookupError as e:
   167→            return _json_response({"error": str(e)}, status=404)
   168→
   169→        return _json_response({"success": True, "message": "Message submitted"})
   170→
   171→    # ── Lifecycle ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   172→
   173→    async def start(self):
   174→        port = _get_port()
   175→        app_runner = web.AppRunner(self.app, access_log=None)
   176→        await app_runner.setup()
   177→        self._site = web.TCPSite(app_runner, "127.0.0.1", port)
   178→        try:
   179→            await self._site.start()
   180→            logger.info("Control API listening on http://127.0.0.1:%d", port)
   181→            self._write_port_file(port)
   182→        except OSError as e:
   183→            logger.warning("Control API failed to bind port %d: %s", port, e)
   184→
   185→    async def stop(self):
   186→        if self._site:
   187→            await self._site.stop()
   188→            self._remove_port_file()
   189→            logger.info("Control API stopped")
   190→
   191→    @staticmethod
   192→    def _write_port_file(port: int):
   193→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   194→        try:
   195→            with open(path, "w") as f:
   196→                f.write(str(port))
   197→        except OSError:
   198→            pass
   199→
   200→    @staticmethod
   201→    def _remove_port_file():
   202→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   203→        try:
   204→            os.remove(path)
   205→        except OSError:
   206→            pass
   207→
```

```
1→"""
     2→Hermes control client — lightweight module for external tools to control
     3→a running Hermes instance (CLI or gateway).
     4→
     5→Usage:
     6→    from gateway.hermes_control_client import HermesControl
     7→
     8→    ctl = HermesControl()  # auto-discovers port from ~/.hermes/control_api.port
     9→
    10→    # Send a message (interrupt mode — stops current work)
    11→    ctl.send_message("Focus on this instead")
    12→
    13→    # Queue a message (processed after current work finishes)
    14→    ctl.send_message("When you're done, also check logs", mode="queue")
    15→
    16→    # Send a slash command
    17→    ctl.send_message("/reset")
    18→    ctl.send_message("/model openrouter:anthropic/claude-sonnet-4")
    19→
    20→    # List running sessions
    21→    sessions = ctl.list_sessions()
    22→
    23→    # List available commands
    24→    commands = ctl.list_commands()
    25→
    26→Zero dependencies beyond stdlib (uses urllib, not requests/aiohttp).
    27→"""
    28→
    29→import json
    30→import os
    31→import urllib.request
    32→import urllib.error
    33→from typing import Optional
    34→
    35→DEFAULT_PORT = 47823
    36→PORT_FILE = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
    37→
    38→
    39→class HermesControl:
    40→    """Client for the Hermes control API."""
    41→
    42→    def __init__(self, host: str = "127.0.0.1", port: Optional[int] = None):
    43→        if port is None:
    44→            port = self._discover_port()
    45→        self.base_url = f"http://{host}:{port}"
    46→
    47→    @staticmethod
    48→    def _discover_port() -> int:
    49→        try:
    50→            with open(PORT_FILE) as f:
    51→                return int(f.read().strip())
    52→        except (OSError, ValueError):
    53→            return DEFAULT_PORT
    54→
    55→    def _request(self, method: str, path: str, body: dict = None) -> dict:
    56→        url = f"{self.base_url}{path}"
    57→        data = json.dumps(body).encode() if body else None
    58→        req = urllib.request.Request(
    59→            url,
    60→            data=data,
    61→            method=method,
    62→            headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"} if data else {},
    63→        )
    64→        try:
    65→            with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=5) as resp:
    66→                return json.loads(resp.read())
    67→        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
    68→            return json.loads(e.read())
    69→        except urllib.error.URLError as e:
    70→            return {"error": f"Cannot reach Hermes control API: {e.reason}"}
    71→
    72→    # ── Read-only endpoints ────────────────────────────────────────────
    73→
    74→    def health(self) -> dict:
    75→        """Check if the control API is running."""
    76→        return self._request("GET", "/health")
    77→
    78→    def list_sessions(self) -> dict:
    79→        """List all running agent sessions."""
    80→        return self._request("GET", "/sessions")
    81→
    82→    def get_session(self, session_key: str = "_any") -> dict:
    83→        """Get info about a specific session."""
    84→        return self._request("GET", f"/sessions/{session_key}")
    85→
    86→    def list_commands(self) -> dict:
    87→        """List available slash commands."""
    88→        return self._request("GET", "/commands")
    89→
    90→    # ── Message injection ──────────────────────────────────────────────
    91→
    92→    def send_message(
    93→        self,
    94→        text: str,
    95→        session_key: str = "_any",
    96→        mode: str = "interrupt",
    97→    ) -> dict:
    98→        """Send a message (or /command) into a running session.
    99→
   100→        Args:
   101→            text: Message text or slash command (e.g., "hello", "/reset").
   102→            session_key: Session to target. "_any" targets the first running session.
   103→            mode: "interrupt" (default) stops current work; "queue" waits for it to finish.
   104→        """
   105→        return self._request("POST", f"/sessions/{session_key}/message", {
   106→            "text": text,
   107→            "mode": mode,
   108→        })
   109→
   110→    # ── Convenience methods ────────────────────────────────────────────
   111→
   112→    def switch_model(self, provider: str, model: str, **kw) -> dict:
   113→        """Switch model via /model command."""
   114→        return self.send_message(f"/model {provider}:{model}", **kw)
   115→
   116→    def compact_context(self, **kw) -> dict:
   117→        """Trigger context compaction."""
   118→        return self.send_message("/compact", **kw)
   119→
   120→    def reset(self, **kw) -> dict:
   121→        """Reset conversation history."""
   122→        return self.send_message("/reset", **kw)
   123→
   124→    def stop(self, **kw) -> dict:
   125→        """Stop the running agent."""
   126→        return self.send_message("/stop", **kw)
   127→
   128→    def is_autoreply_enabled(self, session_key: str = "_any") -> bool:
   129→        """Check if autoreply is enabled for a session."""
   130→        info = self.get_session(session_key)
   131→        return bool(info.get("autoreply", {}).get("enabled"))
   132→
   133→    def is_available(self) -> bool:
   134→        """Check if a Hermes instance with control API is reachable."""
   135→        try:
   136→            return self.health().get("status") == "ok"
   137→        except Exception:
   138→            return False
   139→
```

```
   299→
   300→def get_toolset(name: str) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
   301→    """
   302→    Get a toolset definition by name.
   303→    
   304→    Args:
   305→        name (str): Name of the toolset
   306→        
   307→    Returns:
   308→        Dict: Toolset definition with description, tools, and includes
   309→        None: If toolset not found
   310→    """
   311→    # Return toolset definition
   312→    return TOOLSETS.get(name)
   313→
   314→
   315→def resolve_toolset(name: str, visited: Set[str] = None) -> List[str]:
   316→    """
   317→    Recursively resolve a toolset to get all tool names.
   318→    
   319→    This function handles toolset composition by recursively resolving
   320→    included toolsets and combining all tools.
   321→    
   322→    Args:
   323→        name (str): Name of the toolset to resolve
   324→        visited (Set[str]): Set of already visited toolsets (for cycle detection)
   325→        
   326→    Returns:
   327→        List[str]: List of all tool names in the toolset
   328→    """
   329→    if visited is None:
   330→        visited = set()
   331→    
   332→    # Special aliases that represent all tools across every toolset
   333→    # This ensures future toolsets are automatically included without changes.
   334→    if name in {"all", "*"}:
   335→        all_tools: Set[str] = set()
   336→        for toolset_name in get_toolset_names():
   337→            # Use a fresh visited set per branch to avoid cross-branch contamination
   338→            resolved = resolve_toolset(toolset_name, visited.copy())
   339→            all_tools.update(resolved)
   340→        return list(all_tools)
   341→
   342→    # Check for cycles
   343→    if name in visited:
   344→        print(f"⚠️  Circular dependency detected in toolset '{name}'")
   345→        return []
   346→    
   347→    visited.add(name)
   348→    
   349→    # Get toolset definition
   350→    toolset = TOOLSETS.get(name)
   351→    if not toolset:
   352→        return []
   353→    
   354→    # Collect direct tools
   355→    tools = set(toolset.get("tools", []))
   356→    
   357→    # Recursively resolve included toolsets
   358→    for included_name in toolset.get("includes", []):
   359→        included_tools = resolve_toolset(included_name, visited.copy())
   360→        tools.update(included_tools)
   361→    
   362→    return list(tools)
   363→
   364→
   365→def resolve_multiple_toolsets(toolset_names: List[str]) -> List[str]:
   366→    """
   367→    Resolve multiple toolsets and combine their tools.
   368→    
   369→    Args:
   370→        toolset_names (List[str]): List of toolset names to resolve
   371→        
   372→    Returns:
   373→        List[str]: Combined list of all tool names (deduplicated)
   374→    """
   375→    all_tools = set()
   376→    
   377→    for name in toolset_names:
   378→        tools = resolve_toolset(name)
   379→        all_tools.update(tools)
   380→    
   381→    return list(all_tools)
   382→
   383→
   384→def get_all_toolsets() -> Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]]:
   385→    """
   386→    Get all available toolsets with their definitions.
   387→    
   388→    Returns:
   389→        Dict: All toolset definitions
   390→    """
   391→    return TOOLSETS.copy()
   392→
   393→
   394→def get_toolset_names() -> List[str]:
   395→    """
   396→    Get names of all available toolsets (excluding aliases).
   397→    
   398→    Returns:
   399→        List[str]: List of toolset names
   400→    """
   401→    return list(TOOLSETS.keys())
   402→
   403→
   404→
   405→
   406→def validate_toolset(name: str) -> bool:
   407→    """
   408→    Check if a toolset name is valid.
   409→    
   410→    Args:
   411→        name (str): Toolset name to validate
   412→        
   413→    Returns:
   414→        bool: True if valid, False otherwise
   415→    """
   416→    # Accept special alias names for convenience
   417→    if name in {"all", "*"}:
   418→        return True
   419→    return name in TOOLSETS
   420→
   421→
   422→def create_custom_toolset(
   423→    name: str,
   424→    description: str,
   425→    tools: List[str] = None,
   426→    includes: List[str] = None
   427→) -> None:
   428→    """
   429→    Create a custom toolset at runtime.
   430→    
   431→    Args:
   432→        name (str): Name for the new toolset
   433→        description (str): Description of the toolset
   434→        tools (List[str]): Direct tools to include
   435→        includes (List[str]): Other toolsets to include
   436→    """
   437→    TOOLSETS[name] = {
   438→        "description": description,
   439→        "tools": tools or [],
   440→        "includes": includes or []
   441→    }
   442→
   443→
   444→
   445→
   446→def get_toolset_info(name: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   447→    """
   448→    Get detailed information about a toolset including resolved tools.
   449→    
   450→    Args:
   451→        name (str): Toolset name
   452→        
   453→    Returns:
   454→        Dict: Detailed toolset information
   455→    """
   456→    toolset = get_toolset(name)
   457→    if not toolset:
   458→        return None
   459→    
   460→    resolved_tools = resolve_toolset(name)
   461→    
   462→    return {
   463→        "name": name,
   464→        "description": toolset["description"],
   465→        "direct_tools": toolset["tools"],
   466→        "includes": toolset["includes"],
   467→        "resolved_tools": resolved_tools,
   468→        "tool_count": len(resolved_tools),
   469→        "is_composite": len(toolset["includes"]) > 0
   470→    }
   471→
   472→
   473→def print_toolset_tree(name: str, indent: int = 0) -> None:
   474→    """
   475→    Print a tree view of a toolset and its composition.
   476→    
   477→    Args:
   478→        name (str): Toolset name
   479→        indent (int): Current indentation level
   480→    """
   481→    prefix = "  " * indent
   482→    toolset = get_toolset(name)
   483→    
   484→    if not toolset:
   485→        print(f"{prefix}❌ Unknown toolset: {name}")
   486→        return
   487→    
   488→    # Print toolset name and description
   489→    print(f"{prefix}📦 {name}: {toolset['description']}")
   490→    
   491→    # Print direct tools
   492→    if toolset["tools"]:
   493→        print(f"{prefix}  🔧 Tools: {', '.join(toolset['tools'])}")
   494→    
   495→    # Print included toolsets
   496→    if toolset["includes"]:
   497→        print(f"{prefix}  📂 Includes:")
   498→        for included in toolset["includes"]:
   499→            print_toolset_tree(included, indent + 2)
   500→
   501→
   502→if __name__ == "__main__":
   503→    print("Toolsets System Demo")
   504→    print("=" * 60)
   505→    
   506→    print("\nAvailable Toolsets:")
   507→    print("-" * 40)
   508→    for name, toolset in get_all_toolsets().items():
   509→        info = get_toolset_info(name)
   510→        composite = "[composite]" if info["is_composite"] else "[leaf]"
   511→        print(f"  {composite} {name:20} - {toolset['description']}")
   512→        print(f"     Tools: {len(info['resolved_tools'])} total")
   513→    
   514→    print("\nToolset Resolution Examples:")
   515→    print("-" * 40)
   516→    for name in ["web", "terminal", "safe", "debugging"]:
   517→        tools = resolve_toolset(name)
   518→        print(f"\n  {name}:")
   519→        print(f"    Resolved to {len(tools)} tools: {', '.join(sorted(tools))}")
   520→    
   521→    print("\nMultiple Toolset Resolution:")
   522→    print("-" * 40)
   523→    combined = resolve_multiple_toolsets(["web", "vision", "terminal"])
   524→    print("  Combining ['web', 'vision', 'terminal']:")
   525→    print(f"    Result: {', '.join(sorted(combined))}")
   526→    
   527→    print("\nCustom Toolset Creation:")
   528→    print("-" * 40)
   529→    create_custom_toolset(
   530→        name="my_custom",
   531→        description="My custom toolset for specific tasks",
   532→        tools=["web_search"],
   533→        includes=["terminal", "vision"]
   534→    )
   535→    custom_info = get_toolset_info("my_custom")
   536→    print("  Created 'my_custom' toolset:")
   537→    print(f"    Description: {custom_info['description']}")
   538→    print(f"    Resolved tools: {', '.join(custom_info['resolved_tools'])}")
   539→
```

```
1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""
     3→AI Agent Runner with Tool Calling
     4→
     5→This module provides a clean, standalone agent that can execute AI models
     6→with tool calling capabilities. It handles the conversation loop, tool execution,
     7→and response management.
     8→
     9→Features:
    10→- Automatic tool calling loop until completion
    11→- Configurable model parameters
    12→- Error handling and recovery
    13→- Message history management
    14→- Support for multiple model providers
    15→
    16→Usage:
    17→    from run_agent import AIAgent
    18→    
    19→    agent = AIAgent(base_url="http://localhost:30000/v1", model="claude-opus-4-20250514")
    20→    response = agent.run_conversation("Tell me about the latest Python updates")
```

```
  2685→    # ── Explicit model switch (switch_model tool) ───────────────────────────
  2686→
  2687→    def _switch_model(self, provider: str, model: str) -> dict:
  2688→        """Switch the active model/provider in-place.
  2689→
  2690→        Called by the switch_model tool.  Returns a result dict
  2691→        (success/failure + current state).
  2692→        """
  2693→        provider = provider.strip().lower()
  2694→        model = model.strip()
  2695→
  2696→        old_provider = self.provider
  2697→        old_model = self.model
  2698→
  2699→        try:
  2700→            from agent.auxiliary_client import resolve_provider_credentials
  2701→            creds = resolve_provider_credentials(provider, model)
  2702→            if creds is None:
  2703→                return {
  2704→                    "success": False,
  2705→                    "error": f"Provider '{provider}' is not configured. "
  2706→                             f"Set the appropriate API key or run 'hermes setup'.",
  2707→                    "current": {"provider": old_provider, "model": old_model},
  2708→                }
  2709→
  2710→            self._apply_provider_credentials(creds)
  2711→
  2712→            print(
  2713→                f"{self.log_prefix}🔄 Model switched: "
  2714→                f"{old_model} → {model} via {provider}"
  2715→            )
  2716→            logging.info(
  2717→                "Model switched: %s → %s (%s)",
  2718→                old_model, model, provider,
  2719→            )
  2720→            return {
  2721→                "success": True,
  2722→                "previous": {"provider": old_provider, "model": old_model},
  2723→                "current": {"provider": provider, "model": model},
  2724→                "message": "Model switched successfully",
  2725→            }
  2726→        except Exception as e:
  2727→            logging.error("Failed to switch model: %s", e)
  2728→            return {
  2729→                "success": False,
  2730→                "error": f"Failed to switch model: {e}",
  2731→                "current": {"provider": old_provider, "model": old_model},
  2732→            }
  2733→
  2734→    # ── Control queue ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  2735→    #
  2736→    # External callers (control API, desloppify) send commands via
  2737→    # execute_control(). Immediate commands run in the caller's thread;
  2738→    # deferred commands queue and drain at safe points in the agent loop.
  2739→    #
  2740→    # To add a command: add an entry to _control_handlers and a _ctrl_* method.
  2741→    #   fn:        the method to call (receives **kwargs from JSON + loop context)
  2742→    #   immediate: True = safe to call from any thread (no message context needed)
  2743→    #   external:  False to hide from the HTTP API (default: True / exposed)
  2744→    #
  2745→    # Handler convention: return a notification string → printed with ⚙️ prefix.
  2746→    # Return None if the handler prints its own output (e.g. _switch_model).
  2747→
  2748→    @property
  2749→    def external_control_commands(self) -> list[str]:
  2750→        return [name for name, entry in self._control_handlers.items()
  2751→                if entry.get("external", True)]
  2752→
  2753→    def execute_control(self, command: str, **params) -> dict:
  2754→        """Run a control command. Immediate commands execute now; others queue."""
  2755→        entry = self._control_handlers.get(command)
  2756→        if not entry:
  2757→            return {"success": False, "error": f"Unknown command: '{command}'"}
  2758→        if not entry.get("immediate"):
  2759→            with self._control_lock:
  2760→                self._control_queue.append({"command": command, **params})
  2761→            return {"success": True, "queued": True,
  2762→                    "message": f"'{command}' queued — will apply on next turn"}
  2763→        try:
  2764→            self._run_ctrl(entry["fn"], **params)
  2765→            return {"success": True, "message": f"'{command}' executed"}
  2766→        except Exception as e:
  2767→            logging.error("Control command '%s' failed: %s", command, e)
  2768→            return {"success": False, "error": str(e)}
  2769→
  2770→    def _drain_control_queue(self, messages: list = None,
  2771→                             system_message: str = None, task_id: str = "default"):
  2772→        """Process queued (deferred) commands. Called at safe points in the loop."""
  2773→        with self._control_lock:
  2774→            pending = list(self._control_queue)
  2775→            self._control_queue.clear()
  2776→        ctx = dict(messages=messages, system_message=system_message, task_id=task_id)
  2777→        for cmd in pending:
  2778→            name = cmd.get("command")
  2779→            entry = self._control_handlers.get(name)
  2780→            if not entry:
  2781→                logging.warning("Unknown control command: %s", name)
  2782→                continue
  2783→            params = {k: v for k, v in cmd.items() if k != "command"}
  2784→            try:
  2785→                self._run_ctrl(entry["fn"], **ctx, **params)
  2786→            except Exception as e:
  2787→                logging.error("Control command '%s' failed: %s", name, e)
  2788→
  2789→    def _run_ctrl(self, fn, **kwargs):
  2790→        """Call a handler and print its notification (if any)."""
  2791→        result = fn(**kwargs)
  2792→        if result is not None:
  2793→            print(f"{self.log_prefix}⚙️  {result}")
  2794→
  2795→    # ── Control command implementations ──
  2796→
  2797→    def _ctrl_switch_model(self, provider: str, model: str, **_):
  2798→        self._switch_model(provider, model)
  2799→
  2800→    def _ctrl_interrupt(self, message: str = None, **_):
  2801→        self.interrupt(message)
  2802→
  2803→    def _ctrl_get_status(self, **_):
  2804→        return f"model={self.provider}:{self.model} session={self.session_id} interrupted={self._interrupt_requested}"
  2805→
  2806→    def _ctrl_flush_memories(self, messages: list = None, **_):
  2807→        if not messages:
  2808→            return "Flush skipped: no messages in context yet"
  2809→        self.flush_memories(messages)
  2810→        return "Memories flushed"
  2811→
  2812→    def _ctrl_compact(self, messages: list, system_message: str, task_id: str = "default", **_):
  2813→        if not messages:
  2814→            return "Compact skipped: no messages in context yet"
  2815→        n_before = len(messages)
  2816→        cc = getattr(self, 'context_compressor', None)
  2817→        min_needed = (cc.protect_first_n + cc.protect_last_n + 1) if cc else 7
  2818→        if n_before <= min_needed:
  2819→            return f"Compact skipped: only {n_before} messages (need >{min_needed})"
  2820→        compressed, _ = self._compress_context(messages, system_message, task_id=task_id)
  2821→        messages.clear()
  2822→        messages.extend(compressed)
  2823→        return f"Context compacted: {n_before} → {len(messages)} messages"
  2824→
```

```
  1453→    def _start_control_api(self):
  1454→        """Start the control API in a background thread for external model switching."""
  1455→        try:
  1456→            import asyncio as _aio
  1457→            import threading
  1458→            from gateway.control_api import ControlAPI

  1460→            # Lightweight shim so ControlAPI can find our agent
  1461→            class _CLIRunner:
  1462→                def __init__(self, agent, session_id, cli_instance):
  1463→                    self._running_agents = {session_id: agent}
  1464→                    self._cli = cli_instance
  1465→
  1466→                def inject_message(self, key: str, text: str, *, interrupt: bool = True):
  1467→                    """Inject a message into the CLI's input queues."""
  1468→                    if interrupt and self._cli._agent_running:
  1469→                        self._cli._interrupt_queue.put(text)
  1470→                    else:
  1471→                        self._cli._pending_input.put(text)
  1472→
  1473→                def get_session_info(self, key: str) -> dict:

  1477→                        "autoreply": {
  1478→                            "enabled": cfg is not None,
  1479→                            "prompt": cfg.get("prompt", "") if cfg else None,
  1480→                            "max_turns": cfg.get("max_turns", 0) if cfg else None,
  1481→                            "turn_count": cfg.get("turn_count", 0) if cfg else None,
  1482→                        },
  1483→                    }

  1485→            shim = _CLIRunner(self.agent, self.session_id, self)
  1486→            api = ControlAPI(shim)

  1488→            def _run():
  1489→                try:
  1490→                    loop = _aio.new_event_loop()
  1491→                    _aio.set_event_loop(loop)
  1492→                    loop.run_until_complete(api.start())
  1493→                    loop.run_forever()
  1494→                except Exception as e:
  1495→                    logger.error("Control API thread crashed: %s", e)
  1496→
  1497→            t = threading.Thread(target=_run, daemon=True, name="control-api")
  1498→            t.start()
```

```
1→"""Central registry for all hermes-agent tools.
     2→
     3→Each tool file calls ``registry.register()`` at module level to declare its
     4→schema, handler, toolset membership, and availability check.  ``model_tools.py``
     5→queries the registry instead of maintaining its own parallel data structures.
     6→
     7→Import chain (circular-import safe):
     8→    tools/registry.py  (no imports from model_tools or tool files)
     9→           ^
    10→    tools/*.py  (import from tools.registry at module level)
    11→           ^
    12→    model_tools.py  (imports tools.registry + all tool modules)
    13→           ^
    14→    run_agent.py, cli.py, batch_runner.py, etc.
    15→"""
    16→
    17→import json
    18→import logging
    19→from typing import Callable, Dict, List, Optional, Set
    20→
    21→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    22→
    23→
    24→class ToolEntry:
    25→    """Metadata for a single registered tool."""
    26→
    27→    __slots__ = (
    28→        "name", "toolset", "schema", "handler", "check_fn",
    29→        "requires_env", "is_async", "description",
    30→    )
    31→
    32→    def __init__(self, name, toolset, schema, handler, check_fn,
    33→                 requires_env, is_async, description):
    34→        self.name = name
    35→        self.toolset = toolset
    36→        self.schema = schema
    37→        self.handler = handler
    38→        self.check_fn = check_fn
    39→        self.requires_env = requires_env
    40→        self.is_async = is_async
    41→        self.description = description
    42→
    43→
    44→class ToolRegistry:
    45→    """Singleton registry that collects tool schemas + handlers from tool files."""
    46→
    47→    def __init__(self):
    48→        self._tools: Dict[str, ToolEntry] = {}
    49→        self._toolset_checks: Dict[str, Callable] = {}
    50→
    51→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    52→    # Registration
    53→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    54→
    55→    def register(
    56→        self,
    57→        name: str,
    58→        toolset: str,
    59→        schema: dict,
    60→        handler: Callable,
    61→        check_fn: Callable = None,
    62→        requires_env: list = None,
    63→        is_async: bool = False,
    64→        description: str = "",
    65→    ):
    66→        """Register a tool.  Called at module-import time by each tool file."""
    67→        self._tools[name] = ToolEntry(
    68→            name=name,
    69→            toolset=toolset,
    70→            schema=schema,
    71→            handler=handler,
    72→            check_fn=check_fn,
    73→            requires_env=requires_env or [],
    74→            is_async=is_async,
    75→            description=description or schema.get("description", ""),
    76→        )
    77→        if check_fn and toolset not in self._toolset_checks:
    78→            self._toolset_checks[toolset] = check_fn
    79→
    80→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    81→    # Schema retrieval
    82→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    83→
    84→    def get_definitions(self, tool_names: Set[str], quiet: bool = False) -> List[dict]:
    85→        """Return OpenAI-format tool schemas for the requested tool names.
    86→
    87→        Only tools whose ``check_fn()`` returns True (or have no check_fn)
    88→        are included.
    89→        """
    90→        result = []
    91→        for name in sorted(tool_names):
    92→            entry = self._tools.get(name)
    93→            if not entry:
    94→                continue
    95→            if entry.check_fn:
    96→                try:
    97→                    if not entry.check_fn():
    98→                        if not quiet:
    99→                            logger.debug("Tool %s unavailable (check failed)", name)
   100→                        continue
   101→                except Exception:
   102→                    if not quiet:
   103→                        logger.debug("Tool %s check raised; skipping", name)
   104→                    continue
   105→            result.append({"type": "function", "function": entry.schema})
   106→        return result
   107→
   108→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   109→    # Dispatch
   110→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   111→
   112→    def dispatch(self, name: str, args: dict, **kwargs) -> str:
   113→        """Execute a tool handler by name.
   114→
   115→        * Async handlers are bridged automatically via ``_run_async()``.
   116→        * All exceptions are caught and returned as ``{"error": "..."}``
   117→          for consistent error format.
   118→        """
   119→        entry = self._tools.get(name)
   120→        if not entry:
   121→            return json.dumps({"error": f"Unknown tool: {name}"})
   122→        try:
   123→            if entry.is_async:
   124→                from model_tools import _run_async
   125→                return _run_async(entry.handler(args, **kwargs))
   126→            return entry.handler(args, **kwargs)
   127→        except Exception as e:
   128→            logger.exception("Tool %s dispatch error: %s", name, e)
   129→            return json.dumps({"error": f"Tool execution failed: {type(e).__name__}: {e}"})
   130→
   131→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   132→    # Query helpers  (replace redundant dicts in model_tools.py)
   133→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   134→
   135→    def get_all_tool_names(self) -> List[str]:
   136→        """Return sorted list of all registered tool names."""
   137→        return sorted(self._tools.keys())
   138→
   139→    def get_toolset_for_tool(self, name: str) -> Optional[str]:
   140→        """Return the toolset a tool belongs to, or None."""
   141→        entry = self._tools.get(name)
   142→        return entry.toolset if entry else None
   143→
   144→    def get_tool_to_toolset_map(self) -> Dict[str, str]:
   145→        """Return ``{tool_name: toolset_name}`` for every registered tool."""
   146→        return {name: e.toolset for name, e in self._tools.items()}
   147→
   148→    def is_toolset_available(self, toolset: str) -> bool:
   149→        """Check if a toolset's requirements are met.
   150→
   151→        Returns False (rather than crashing) when the check function raises
   152→        an unexpected exception (e.g. network error, missing import, bad config).
   153→        """
   154→        check = self._toolset_checks.get(toolset)
   155→        if not check:
   156→            return True
   157→        try:
   158→            return bool(check())
   159→        except Exception:
   160→            logger.debug("Toolset %s check raised; marking unavailable", toolset)
   161→            return False
   162→
   163→    def check_toolset_requirements(self) -> Dict[str, bool]:
   164→        """Return ``{toolset: available_bool}`` for every toolset."""
   165→        toolsets = set(e.toolset for e in self._tools.values())
   166→        return {ts: self.is_toolset_available(ts) for ts in sorted(toolsets)}
   167→
   168→    def get_available_toolsets(self) -> Dict[str, dict]:
   169→        """Return toolset metadata for UI display."""
   170→        toolsets: Dict[str, dict] = {}
   171→        for entry in self._tools.values():
   172→            ts = entry.toolset
   173→            if ts not in toolsets:
   174→                toolsets[ts] = {
   175→                    "available": self.is_toolset_available(ts),
   176→                    "tools": [],
   177→                    "description": "",
   178→                    "requirements": [],
   179→                }
   180→            toolsets[ts]["tools"].append(entry.name)
   181→            if entry.requires_env:
   182→                for env in entry.requires_env:
   183→                    if env not in toolsets[ts]["requirements"]:
   184→                        toolsets[ts]["requirements"].append(env)
   185→        return toolsets
   186→
   187→    def get_toolset_requirements(self) -> Dict[str, dict]:
   188→        """Build a TOOLSET_REQUIREMENTS-compatible dict for backward compat."""
   189→        result: Dict[str, dict] = {}
   190→        for entry in self._tools.values():
   191→            ts = entry.toolset
   192→            if ts not in result:
   193→                result[ts] = {
   194→                    "name": ts,
   195→                    "env_vars": [],
   196→                    "check_fn": self._toolset_checks.get(ts),
   197→                    "setup_url": None,
   198→                    "tools": [],
   199→                }
   200→            if entry.name not in result[ts]["tools"]:
   201→                result[ts]["tools"].append(entry.name)
   202→            for env in entry.requires_env:
   203→                if env not in result[ts]["env_vars"]:
   204→                    result[ts]["env_vars"].append(env)
   205→        return result
   206→
   207→    def check_tool_availability(self, quiet: bool = False):
   208→        """Return (available_toolsets, unavailable_info) like the old function."""
   209→        available = []
   210→        unavailable = []
   211→        seen = set()
   212→        for entry in self._tools.values():
   213→            ts = entry.toolset
   214→            if ts in seen:
   215→                continue
   216→            seen.add(ts)
   217→            if self.is_toolset_available(ts):
   218→                available.append(ts)
   219→            else:
   220→                unavailable.append({
   221→                    "name": ts,
   222→                    "env_vars": entry.requires_env,
   223→                    "tools": [e.name for e in self._tools.values() if e.toolset == ts],
   224→                })
   225→        return available, unavailable
   226→
   227→
   228→# Module-level singleton
   229→registry = ToolRegistry()
   230→
```

```
1→"""
     2→Gateway configuration management.
     3→
     4→Handles loading and validating configuration for:
     5→- Connected platforms (Telegram, Discord, WhatsApp)
     6→- Home channels for each platform
     7→- Session reset policies
     8→- Delivery preferences

    10→
    11→import logging
    12→import os

    14→from pathlib import Path
    15→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    16→from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Any

    19→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

    21→
    22→class Platform(Enum):
    23→    """Supported messaging platforms."""
    24→    LOCAL = "local"
    25→    TELEGRAM = "telegram"
    26→    DISCORD = "discord"
    27→    WHATSAPP = "whatsapp"
    28→    SLACK = "slack"

    34→@dataclass
    35→class HomeChannel:
    36→    """
    37→    Default destination for a platform.
    38→    
    39→    When a cron job specifies deliver="telegram" without a specific chat ID,
    40→    messages are sent to this home channel.
    41→    """
    42→    platform: Platform

    44→    name: str  # Human-readable name for display

    47→        return {
    48→            "platform": self.platform.value,
    49→            "chat_id": self.chat_id,
    50→            "name": self.name,
    51→        }
    52→    
    53→    @classmethod
    54→    def from_dict(cls, data: Dict[str, Any]) -> "HomeChannel":

    56→            platform=Platform(data["platform"]),
    57→            chat_id=str(data["chat_id"]),
    58→            name=data.get("name", "Home"),
    59→        )
```

```
255→# handle_function_call  (the main dispatcher)
   256→# =============================================================================
   257→
   258→# Tools whose execution is intercepted by the agent loop (run_agent.py)
   259→# because they need agent-level state (TodoStore, MemoryStore, etc.).
   260→# The registry still holds their schemas; dispatch just returns a stub error
   261→# so if something slips through, the LLM sees a sensible message.
   262→_AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS = {"todo", "memory", "session_search", "delegate_task", "switch_model", "smart_model"}
   263→
   264→
   265→def handle_function_call(
   266→    function_name: str,
   267→    function_args: Dict[str, Any],
   268→    task_id: Optional[str] = None,
   269→    user_task: Optional[str] = None,
   270→    enabled_tools: Optional[List[str]] = None,
   271→) -> str:
   272→    """
   273→    Main function call dispatcher that routes calls to the tool registry.
   274→
   275→    Args:
   276→        function_name: Name of the function to call.
   277→        function_args: Arguments for the function.
   278→        task_id: Unique identifier for terminal/browser session isolation.
   279→        user_task: The user's original task (for browser_snapshot context).
   280→        enabled_tools: Tool names enabled for this session.  When provided,
   281→                       execute_code uses this list to determine which sandbox
   282→                       tools to generate.  Falls back to the process-global
   283→                       ``_last_resolved_tool_names`` for backward compat.
   284→
```

I now have all the information needed. Here is the detailed implementation plan.

---

## Implementation Plan: `run_command` Tool + Control Queue Removal

### Overview

This refactor replaces two special-purpose agent tools (`switch_model`, `smart_model`) and the internal control queue mechanism with a single `run_command` tool that calls the agent's own local HTTP control API. The agent becomes a client of itself, using the same `POST /sessions/_any/message` endpoint that external tools already use.

---

### Phase 1: Create the `run_command` tool

**New file: `tools/run_command_tool.py`**

This tool lets the agent execute any slash command by HTTP-posting to `127.0.0.1:{port}/sessions/_any/message`. It replaces both `switch_model` and `smart_model`.

Key design decisions:
- Use `urllib` (like `gateway/hermes_control_client.py` does) -- zero extra dependencies.
- The `check_fn` gates on an env var `HERMES_SELF_COMMAND=1` (or config flag). When not set, the tool is not exposed to the model.
- Port discovery reuses the same logic from `gateway/hermes_control_client.py:48-51` (read `~/.hermes/control_api.port`, fall back to `DEFAULT_PORT=47823`).
- The tool schema exposes two parameters: `command` (the slash command string, e.g. `/model openrouter:anthropic/claude-sonnet-4`) and `mode` (interrupt vs queue, defaulting to queue since the agent is sending commands to itself during its own run).

Schema pattern to follow: `tools/switch_model_tool.py` (lines 70-101 for schema, 111-121 for registry.register).

The handler does NOT need agent-level state -- it makes an HTTP call. So unlike `switch_model`/`smart_model`, it should NOT be added to `_AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS` in `model_tools.py`. The registry handler itself is the real handler.

**Implementation details:**
- Import `gateway.hermes_control_client.HermesControl` or inline the HTTP call (inlining is simpler and avoids coupling).
- `check_fn`: `lambda: os.getenv("HERMES_SELF_COMMAND", "").lower() in ("1", "true", "yes")`
- Register with `toolset="run_command"` so it gets its own toolset entry.

   ```
   "switch_model",
   "smart_model",
   ```
   with:
   ```
   "run_command",
   ```

2. **Lines 107-117**: Remove the `"switch_model"` and `"smart_model"` toolset entries. Add:
   ```python
   "run_command": {
       "description": "Execute slash commands via the local control API (self-command)",
       "tools": ["run_command"],
       "includes": []
   },
   ```


2. **Line 262**: In `_AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS`, remove `"switch_model"` and `"smart_model"`. Do NOT add `"run_command"` (it handles itself via HTTP, no agent-level state needed).

   The `run_command` tool is safe to run concurrently (it makes an HTTP call), but you may want to add `"run_command"` to this guard instead, since executing multiple commands simultaneously could cause races. Decision: add `"run_command"` to the sequential guard.

   - Test that the tool registers in the registry.
   - Test that `check_fn` returns False when `HERMES_SELF_COMMAND` is not set.
   - Test that `check_fn` returns True when `HERMES_SELF_COMMAND=1`.
   - Test that the handler makes an HTTP POST to the correct URL.
   - Test error handling when the control API is unreachable.

3. **Verify** existing control API tests at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/tests/gateway/test_control_api.py` still pass (they test `inject_message` which is unchanged).

### Phase 10: Gate with `self_command` flag

The gating mechanism uses the `check_fn` pattern already established in the codebase (see `tools/homeassistant_tool.py:321` for `_check_ha_available`, `tools/send_message_tool.py:318` for `_check_send_message`).

In `tools/run_command_tool.py`:
```python
def _check_run_command() -> bool:
    return os.getenv("HERMES_SELF_COMMAND", "").lower() in ("1", "true", "yes")
```

When `HERMES_SELF_COMMAND` is not set, the tool will not appear in the agent's tool list (the registry's `get_definitions` skips tools whose `check_fn` returns False -- see `tools/registry.py:84-106`).

---

### Dependency Order

1. Create `tools/run_command_tool.py` (Phase 1)
2. Update `toolsets.py` (Phase 2)
3. Update `model_tools.py` (Phase 3)
4. Remove control queue from `run_agent.py` (Phase 4)
5. Remove special-case dispatch from `run_agent.py` (Phase 5)
6. Update prompt builder (Phase 6)
7. Delete old tool files (Phase 7)
8. Clean up stray references (Phase 8)
9. Update tests (Phase 9)

Phases 1-3 can be done first and tested independently (both old and new tools coexist). Then phases 4-7 are the breaking changes. Phase 8-9 are cleanup.

### Risks and Considerations

- **Latency**: The old `switch_model` tool called `agent._switch_model()` directly (in-process). The new `run_command` tool makes an HTTP round-trip through the control API. This adds a few milliseconds but is negligible for a tool call.

- **Control API must be running**: The `run_command` tool depends on the control API being active. In CLI mode, this is started at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py:1453-1498`. In gateway mode, it is started in `gateway/run.py`. If neither is running, the tool will return an error. The `check_fn` gating on `HERMES_SELF_COMMAND` ensures users opt in.

- **Deferred commands (compact, flush_memories)**: The old control queue had "deferred" commands that waited for safe drain points in the loop. With the control API path, `inject_message` uses the interrupt/queue mode. A `/compact` command sent via `run_command` with `mode=queue` will be processed after the current turn, which achieves the same effect. This is safe.

- **`_switch_model()` method stays**: Even though `switch_model_tool.py` is deleted, the `_switch_model()` method on `AIAgent` (lines 2687-2732) is still needed. The CLI's `/model` slash command handler calls it directly. The control API path goes: HTTP POST -> `inject_message` -> CLI input queue -> `/model` command handler -> `_switch_model()`.

```
{"body":"## Summary\n\nEvery context compression event silently drops conversation history instead of summarizing it. The summary model call always fails with:\n\n```\nFailed to generate context summary with auxiliary model: Responses.stream() got an unexpected keyword argument 'stream'\n```\n\nThe fallback to the main model never runs, so `_generate_summary()` returns `None` on every compression — 44 times across two days in my logs.\n\n## Root Cause\n\n`_call_summary_model()` in `agent/context_compressor.py` uses `client.chat.completions.create()`. But when the auxiliary client resolves to the Codex path (`chatgpt.com/backend-api/codex`), it returns a client backed by the **Responses API**, not Chat Completions. The Responses API has a different interface and rejects the `stream=` keyword argument, crashing the call before the fallback logic in `_generate_summary()` can run.\n\n## Steps to Reproduce\n\n1. Use default config (`summary_model: gpt-5.1-codex-mini`, `summary_provider: auto`)\n2. Let a session grow until compression triggers (200 msgs or token threshold)\n3. Check `~/.hermes/logs/gateway.log`\n\n## Expected Behavior\n\nCompression should fall back to the main model when the auxiliary client fails, producing a real summary instead of dropping context.\n\n## Actual Behavior\n\nBoth the auxiliary model and fallback silently fail (fallback never actually runs after the Responses API crash). Middle turns are deleted with no summary. From my logs, every single compression over two days hit this path — **44 compression events, all resulting in silent context loss**.\n\nExample log sequence:\n```\nWARNING: Failed to generate context summary with auxiliary model: Responses.stream() got an unexpected keyword argument 'stream'\nWARNING: Context compression: no model available for summary. Middle turns will be dropped without summary.\nINFO: Session hygiene: compressed 283 → 7 msgs, ~154,456 → ~2,507 tokens\n```\n\n## Environment\n\n- Default `cli-config.yaml` settings (`summary_model: gpt-5.1-codex-mini`, `summary_provider: auto`)\n- Main model: `gpt-5.4` via OpenAI API\n- No Codex OAuth token configured\n- Gateway log: compression firing every 5–15 minutes during active sessions\n\n## Notes\n\nThis is separate from the triplication bug fixed in #873. That fix is great and already merged — this is a second independent issue in the compression path.","labels":[],"title":"Context compression silently drops history due to Responses API / stream kwarg mismatch in auxiliary client"}
```

```
{"body":"Impressive agent — the self-improving loop and subagent delegation model are exactly the right patterns.\n\nWanted to share **[GNAP](https://github.com/farol-team/gnap)** (Git-Native Agent Protocol) as a coordination layer that could complement Hermes's subagent spawning.\n\n**The gap GNAP fills:**\nHermes spawns isolated subagents for parallel workstreams. Currently, coordination between those subagents (task handoffs, shared state, completion signaling) happens in-process or not at all. GNAP externalizes this to a shared git repo.\n\n**Why git for subagent coordination:**\n- Subagents on different machines can coordinate (git is universal)\n- Persistent state survives subagent crashes\n- Full audit trail of who did what and when\n- No central server — just a repo any subagent can clone\n\n```bash\n# Main agent spawns subagents via GNAP\ngnap create-task --title \"Research competitors\" --assigned-to subagent-1\ngnap create-task --title \"Write report\" --assigned-to subagent-2 --depends-on task-1\n\n# Subagent 1 picks up task\ngit pull && cat gnap/tasks/task-1.json  # → status: pending\n# ... does work ...\ngnap complete-task task-1 --output research-results.md\n\n# Subagent 2 sees dependency resolved, starts automatically\n```\n\n**Fits naturally with Hermes's design:**\n- Hermes already uses git (OpenClaw migration notes git)\n- GNAP adds structured task coordination on top of git you already have\n- Works with any backend terminal (local, Docker, SSH, Modal)\n\nRepo: https://github.com/farol-team/gnap — thoughts?","labels":[],"title":"GNAP: persistent coordination layer for parallel subagent workstreams"}
```

tool_result Bash

```
{"body":"## Summary\n\nWhen a user runs `hermes setup` or `hermes model` while the gateway is running (e.g. on Home Assistant, Telegram, Discord), there's a race condition that can cause the gateway to send requests with an incompatible model name to the new provider.\n\n## Root Cause\n\n`_update_config_for_provider()` in `hermes_cli/auth.py` (line ~1544) writes the new provider and base_url to `config.yaml` **immediately** — before model selection happens.\n\nIn the MiniMax setup flow (`setup.py` lines 1036-1037):\n```python\n_update_config_for_provider(\"minimax\", pconfig.inference_base_url)  # writes to disk NOW\n_set_model_provider(config, \"minimax\", pconfig.inference_base_url)\n# ... model selection happens much later at line ~1278\n```\n\nSince the gateway re-reads config.yaml per-message, this creates a window where:\n- `model.provider` = `minimax`, `model.base_url` = `https://api.minimax.io/v1`\n- `model.default` = `anthropic/claude-opus-4.6` (unchanged from previous provider)\n\nThe gateway then sends `anthropic/claude-opus-4.6` to MiniMax's API, which doesn't serve that model.\n\n## Reproduction\n\n1. Configure OpenRouter with `anthropic/claude-opus-4.6` (default)\n2. Start the gateway (`hermes gateway run`)\n3. Chat via Home Assistant / Telegram — works fine\n4. In a separate terminal, run `hermes setup` or `hermes model`\n5. Select MiniMax as provider, enter API key\n6. **Before completing model selection**, send a message via the gateway\n7. The gateway picks up MiniMax as provider but still uses the Claude model name → fails\n\nEven without the race: if the user selects \"Keep current\" at the model selection step, the model stays as `anthropic/claude-opus-4.6` permanently — which is always wrong for non-OpenRouter providers.\n\n## Affected Providers\n\nAny non-OpenRouter provider where the model name format differs: MiniMax, MiniMax-CN, Z.AI, Kimi, Anthropic (native). The OpenRouter-formatted model name (`anthropic/claude-opus-4.6`) won't work on these endpoints.\n\n## Suggested Fixes\n\n**Option A (minimal):** Don't write config.yaml until the full setup flow completes. Buffer provider + base_url changes in memory, only flush to disk after model selection.\n\n**Option B (defensive):** When `_update_config_for_provider` changes the provider, also set `model.default` to the first model in that provider's default list (e.g. `MiniMax-M2.5` for minimax). The user can still change it during model selection, but at least the intermediate state is valid.\n\n**Option C (validation):** Add a gateway-side sanity check: if the resolved provider is not `openrouter` and the model name contains a `/` prefix (OpenRouter format), log a warning and refuse to start the agent until the config is fixed.\n\n## User Report\n\nReported by a Discord user (Hunter) running Hermes on Home Assistant. His session log showed `model: anthropic/claude-opus-4.6` with `base_url: https://api.minimax.io/v1` — the agent appeared to \"switch providers mid-conversation\" from his perspective.","labels":[],"title":"bug: setup flow writes provider to config.yaml before model selection, causing gateway race condition"}
```

tool_result Bash

```
{"body":"## Problem\n\nIn Discord channels, Telegram groups, and other non-DM contexts, **all users share a single session**. The session key is constructed as `agent:main:{platform}:{chat_type}:{chat_id}` (in `gateway/session.py` `build_session_key()`), meaning every message from every user in that channel feeds into one conversation history.\n\nThis causes context to balloon much faster than single-user DMs, making it significantly more likely to hit the context limit — especially in active Discord servers where multiple people may be chatting with the bot concurrently.\n\nThis was a contributing factor to #813 — the session hit 233k tokens in a Discord conversation.\n\n## Current Behavior\n\n- All users in a Discord channel/group share one conversation context\n- User A asks a complex question triggering 10+ tool calls → context grows by ~50k tokens\n- User B asks an unrelated question → gets all of User A's context plus their own\n- Multiple users active in the same channel can push context past the limit within a few exchanges\n- The interrupt mechanism (`_running_agents` / `_pending_messages`) means if two users message at the same time, one interrupts the other's agent run\n\n## Impact\n\n1. **Context overflow**: Shared sessions hit the context limit much faster than single-user sessions\n2. **Confused responses**: The agent sees all users' conversations as one thread — it may reference User A's request when responding to User B\n3. **Interrupt conflicts**: If User A's agent is running and User B sends a message, User B's message interrupts User A's agent and gets queued as a pending message\n4. **Cost**: Every user's message rehydrates the entire shared context, paying for tokens from other users' conversations\n\n## Possible Solutions\n\n### Option A: Per-user sessions in group contexts\nKey sessions by `platform:chat_type:chat_id:user_id` instead of just `platform:chat_type:chat_id` for group/channel contexts. Each user gets their own conversation thread.\n\n**Pros:** Clean isolation, no cross-contamination\n**Cons:** Agent loses shared context (can't reference what another user said), uses more storage\n\n### Option B: More aggressive compression thresholds for group sessions\nKeep shared sessions but lower the compression threshold (e.g., 60% instead of 85%) for group contexts, and compress more aggressively.\n\n**Pros:** Simple, preserves shared context\n**Cons:** Doesn't solve the confused-responses problem\n\n### Option C: Thread-based sessions for Discord\nIn Discord specifically, encourage/require thread usage — each thread gets its own session (already works since thread_id is part of the source). The main channel could auto-create threads per interaction.\n\n**Pros:** Natural UX for Discord, clean isolation\n**Cons:** Platform-specific, doesn't help Telegram/WhatsApp groups\n\n### Option D: Hybrid — shared context with user-tagged messages  \nKeep one session but tag each message with the user who sent it. The system prompt could instruct the agent to track who said what and respond accordingly.\n\n**Pros:** Natural group conversation, agent can reference cross-user context\n**Cons:** Still shares context window, still has the interrupt problem\n\n## Environment\n\n- `gateway/session.py` `build_session_key()` — line 293\n- `gateway/run.py` `_handle_message()` — interrupt handling at line 757\n- Observed on Discord with multiple users in a DM group\n","labels":[],"title":"Group/channel sessions shared across all users — context bloats fast with multiple active users"}
```

```
{"body":"### Bug Description\n\nThere is a critical privacy and context-bleeding bug in the platform gateway. \nCurrently, the build_session_key() function assumes that only WhatsApp uses a chat_id for Direct Messages (DMs). If users DM the bot on Telegram or Discord, the agent strips the chat_id entirely and falls back to a global session key (e.g., agent:main:telegram:dm). \nThis means every single person who DMs the bot on Telegram is funneled into one massive, shared session. The agent sees a live feed of everyone's private DMs interwoven in a single context window. Group chats are unaffected because they hit a different return statement.\n\n### Steps to Reproduce\n\n1. Start the Hermes agent with the Telegram (or Discord) gateway enabled.\n2. Send a DM to the bot from User A.\n3. Send a DM to the bot from User B.\n4. Check the agent's context/memory or look at ~/.hermes/sessions/sessions.json. \n\n### Expected Behavior\n\nAll Direct Messages, regardless of the platform, should be isolated by their respective chat_id (e.g., agent:main:telegram:dm:<chat_id>). User A and User B should have completely separate, private sessions.\n\n### Actual Behavior\n\nBoth users are sharing the exact same session file/key (agent:main:telegram:dm), rather than having isolated sessions.\n\n### Affected Component\n\nGateway (Telegram/Discord/Slack/WhatsApp)\n\n### Messaging Platform (if gateway-related)\n\nTelegram\n\n### Operating System\n\nUbuntu 24.04\n\n### Python Version\n\n3.11.15\n\n### Hermes Version\n\nvv1.0.0\n\n### Relevant Logs / Traceback\n\n```shell\n(No error traceback, but you will see the logs showing the shared session key:)\n[gateway] Loading transcript for agent:main:telegram:dm\n[gateway] Saving session index...\n```\n\n### Root Cause Analysis (optional)\n\nIn hermes-agent/gateway/session.py, build_session_key() explicitly hardcodes WhatsApp as the only multi-user DM platform around line 304:\n\n    platform = source.platform.value\n    if source.chat_type == \"dm\":\n        if platform == \"whatsapp\" and source.chat_id:\n            return f\"agent:main:{platform}:dm:{source.chat_id}\"\n        return f\"agent:main:{platform}:dm\"\n\nBecause of this if platform == \"whatsapp\" check, all other platforms fall through to return f\"agent:main:{platform}:dm\", which completely strips the chat_id and causes the context bleeding.\n\n### Proposed Fix (optional)\n\nI have already patched this locally and will be opening a Pull Request shortly. The fix simplifies the logic to universally isolate by chat_id whenever it is present, regardless of platform or chat type, before falling back:\n    platform = source.platform.value\n    \n    # Always isolate sessions by chat_id, regardless of platform or chat_type\n    if source.chat_id:\n        if source.thread_id:\n            return f\"agent:main:{platform}:{source.chat_type}:{source.chat_id}:{source.thread_id}\"\n        return f\"agent:main:{platform}:{source.chat_type}:{source.chat_id}\"\n        \n    # Fallback only if chat_id is somehow missing\n    return f\"agent:main:{platform}:{source.chat_type}\"\n\n(Also requires a one-line update to tests/gateway/test_session.py::TestWhatsAppDMSessionKeyConsistency.test_telegram_dm_omits_chat_id)\n\n### Are you willing to submit a PR for this?\n\n- [x] I'd like to fix this myself and submit a PR","labels":[{"id":"LA_kwDOPRF1G88AAAACF7GHnA","name":"bug","description":"Something isn't working","color":"d73a4a"}],"title":"[Bug]: Telegram and Discord DMs share a single global session (Context Bleeding)"}
```

```
{"body":"## Summary\n\nEvery message in a gateway session gets written to the SQLite database (`state.db`) multiple times, causing the loaded conversation history to be 3–4x larger than it should be. This results in proportionally inflated token usage on every API call.\n\nThe JSONL transcript file is unaffected and contains the correct message count.\n\n## Root Cause\n\nThree separate code paths all write to the same SQLite session, with no deduplication:\n\n**1. `run_agent._log_msg_to_db()`** (in `run_agent.py`)\nCalled immediately when the user message is appended to the messages list. Writes the user message once.\n\n**2. `run_agent._persist_session()` → `_flush_messages_to_session_db()`** (in `run_agent.py`)\nCalled at every exit point in the tool-calling loop — normal finish, errors, retries, early returns (~15+ call sites). Each call re-flushes all new messages from `start_idx = len(conversation_history)` with no check for what was already written. For a normal conversation turn with tool calls, this fires multiple times.\n\n**3. `gateway.session_store.append_to_transcript()`** (in `gateway/run.py`, `_handle_message`)\nCalled after the agent returns, writing the new messages again via the gateway's own `SessionStore._db`.\n\n`GatewayRunner._session_db` and `SessionStore._db` are separate `SessionDB()` instances but both connect to the same `state.db` file.\n\n## Result\n\n- User messages: written ~3x (paths 1 + 2 + 3)\n- Assistant/tool messages: written ~2–3x (paths 2 + 3, potentially multiple times via path 2)\n- `load_transcript()` prefers SQLite over JSONL, so it loads the inflated history on every message\n- Observed ratio in practice: ~3.4x (194 messages in JSONL, 654 in SQLite for the same session)\n\n## Reproduction\n\nRun a gateway session (Telegram/Discord), send ~10 messages with tool calls, then:\n\n```python\nimport sqlite3\nconn = sqlite3.connect('~/.hermes/state.db')\nc = conn.cursor()\nc.execute(\"SELECT role, COUNT(*) FROM messages WHERE session_id=? GROUP BY role\", (session_id,))\nprint(c.fetchall())\n```\n\nCompare against the JSONL line count for the same session. SQLite count will be ~3x higher.\n\n## Impact\n\nToken usage is inflated by 3–4x per message. A session with a 5-hour usage budget was exhausted in ~40 minutes of actual conversation.\n\n## Suggested Fix\n\n**Option A (minimal):** Track a `_last_flushed_db_idx` in `_flush_messages_to_session_db` so repeat calls only write truly new messages:\n\n```python\ndef _flush_messages_to_session_db(self, messages, conversation_history=None):\n    if not self._session_db:\n        return\n    start_idx = getattr(self, '_last_flushed_db_idx', len(conversation_history) if conversation_history else 0)\n    for msg in messages[start_idx:]:\n        self._session_db.append_message(...)\n    self._last_flushed_db_idx = len(messages)\n```\n\n**Option B:** Remove `_log_msg_to_db` from `run_conversation` (redundant with `_flush_messages_to_session_db`) and ensure the gateway's `append_to_transcript` doesn't double-write what `_flush_messages_to_session_db` already persisted.\n\n**Option C (quickest workaround):** Swap `load_transcript` priority to prefer JSONL over SQLite, since the JSONL is always correct.\n\n## Environment\n\n- Commit: `f6bc620`\n- Platform: Arch Linux, gateway via systemd user service\n- Platforms affected: Telegram (confirmed), likely all gateway platforms","labels":[],"title":"bug: SQLite session transcript accumulates duplicate messages (3-4x token inflation)"}
```
