```
{"body":"Bug\n\n  When the agent is connected via the gateway (e.g. Telegram), calling the session_search tool returns:\n\n  {\"error\": \"session_search must be handled by the agent loop\"}\n\n  Root Cause\n\n  gateway/run.py creates AIAgent(...) without passing session_db=, so self._session_db is None in the agent instance.\n\n  The tool dispatch in run_agent.py has this condition:\n\n  elif function_name == \"session_search\" and self._session_db:\n\n  When _session_db is None, the branch is skipped entirely and execution falls through to handle_function_call() in model_tools.py, which returns\n  the generic error for all tools in _AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS:\n\n  _AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS = {\"todo\", \"memory\", \"session_search\", \"delegate_task\"}\n\n  def handle_function_call(...):\n      if function_name in _AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS:\n          return json.dumps({\"error\": f\"{function_name} must be handled by the agent loop\"})\n\n  The CLI path works because cli.py initializes and passes the DB:\n\n  from hermes_state import SessionDB\n  self._session_db = SessionDB()\n  # ...\n  agent = AIAgent(..., session_db=self._session_db)\n\n  Fix\n\n  Two changes needed:\n\n  1. gateway/run.py — Pass session_db to AIAgent\n\n  Initialize SessionDB in GatewayRunner.__init__():\n\n  self._session_db = None\n  try:\n      from hermes_state import SessionDB\n      self._session_db = SessionDB()\n  except Exception as e:\n      logger.debug(\"SQLite session store not available: %s\", e)\n\n  And pass it in run_sync():\n\n  agent = AIAgent(\n      ...,\n      session_db=self._session_db,\n  )\n\n  2. run_agent.py — Defensive fallback when _session_db is None\n\n  Change the dispatch branch to always intercept session_search and return a clear error if the DB is unavailable, instead of falling through:\n\n  elif function_name == \"session_search\":\n      if not self._session_db:\n          function_result = json.dumps({\"success\": False, \"error\": \"Session database not available.\"})\n      else:\n          from tools.session_search_tool import session_search as _session_search\n          function_result = _session_search(\n              query=function_args.get(\"query\", \"\"),\n              role_filter=function_args.get(\"role_filter\"),\n              limit=function_args.get(\"limit\", 3),\n              db=self._session_db,\n          )\n\n  Affected Files\n\n  - gateway/run.py — GatewayRunner.__init__() and run_sync()\n  - run_agent.py — tool dispatch block (~line 1462)","labels":[],"title":"Gateway doesn't pass session_db to AIAgent, causing \"session_search must be handled by the agent loop\" error"}
```

```
{"body":"## Summary\nCurrently, when Hermes is busy processing a task and the user sends a new message, the system either:\n1. Interrupts the current task (current behavior)\n2. Queues the follow-up messages (PR #1344 approach)\n\nThis feature request proposes a third option: **parallel task execution** where Hermes can spawn separate tasks to handle concurrent user requests simultaneously.\n\n## Use Case\nAs a user, I want to be able to:\n- Ask Hermes to apply a code patch (long-running task)\n- Simultaneously ask Hermes to generate an image (independent task)\n- Have both tasks execute in parallel without blocking each other\n\n## Benefits\n- **Improved user experience**: No waiting for long tasks to complete before starting new ones\n- **Better resource utilization**: Independent tasks can run concurrently\n- **Natural workflow**: Matches how users interact with AI assistants - multiple requests in flight\n\n## Potential Implementation Approaches\n\n### Option 1: Conversation-Level Parallelism\n- Each conversation maintains a task queue\n- User can spawn multiple independent tasks within the same conversation\n- Tasks are isolated but share conversation context\n\n### Option 2: Session-Level Parallelism  \n- Each new request spawns a sub-agent with isolated context\n- Sub-agents report back when complete\n- Parent agent coordinates results\n\n### Option 3: Tool-Level Parallelism\n- Certain tools (image generation, search) run in background\n- Main agent continues processing\n- Results delivered when ready\n\n## Questions to Consider\n1. How should conversation context be managed across parallel tasks?\n2. Should there be a limit on concurrent tasks per user?\n3. How to handle resource contention (e.g., both tasks want to edit the same file)?\n4. Should the user be able to \"switch\" between active tasks?\n\n## Related\n- PR #1344 (Message Queuing) - This is a complementary feature, not a replacement\n- This addresses a different use case: concurrent independent tasks vs. sequential follow-ups\n\n---\n*This feature request stems from discussion in PR #1344*","labels":[],"title":"Feature Request: Parallel Task Execution for Concurrent User Requests"}
```

```
{"body":"## Overview\n\nDeerFlow (ByteDance's open-source SuperAgent, [github.com/bytedance/deer-flow](https://github.com/bytedance/deer-flow)) implements a formal middleware architecture for the agent loop — an ordered chain of middleware components with lifecycle hooks (`before_agent`, `after_agent`, `before_model`, `after_model`, `wrap_model_call`, `wrap_tool_call`). Each middleware declares its own compatible state subset and can intercept, modify, or augment the agent's behavior at precise points in the execution lifecycle.\n\nTwo middlewares are particularly interesting for Hermes:\n\n1. **DanglingToolCallMiddleware** — automatically detects and repairs broken conversation histories where AI messages have `tool_calls` but no corresponding `ToolMessage` responses (a common cause of API errors and confused behavior).\n\n2. **SubagentLimitMiddleware** — hard-enforces subagent concurrency limits by **rewriting the AI's message** to truncate excess `task` tool calls, providing defense-in-depth beyond prompt-only soft limits.\n\nHermes currently has an event-driven hook system (`gateway/hooks.py`) and various ad-hoc interception patterns (dangerous command approval, memory nudge injection, content scanning), but no formal middleware chain. Formalizing this would consolidate these scattered patterns and enable new ones like the two above.\n\nRelated: #520 (Tool Call Repair Middleware), #359 (Enhanced Extension System), #512 (Doom Loop Detection), #481 (Tool-Call Loop Guard)\n\n---\n\n## Research Findings\n\n### DeerFlow's Middleware Architecture\n\nDeerFlow uses LangChain's `AgentMiddleware[StateType]` base class. Each middleware overrides specific lifecycle hooks and declares a compatible state schema.\n\n**Middleware ordering is explicit and documented:**\n\n```python\ndef _build_middlewares(config, model_name, agent_name=None):\n    middlewares = [\n        ThreadDataMiddleware(),       # MUST be first (sets up thread_id)\n        UploadsMiddleware(),          # After ThreadData (needs thread_id)\n        SandboxMiddleware(),          # After ThreadData\n        DanglingToolCallMiddleware(), # Before model calls\n    ]\n    # Conditionally add: summarization, title, memory, view_image, subagent_limit\n    middlewares.append(ClarificationMiddleware())  # MUST be last\n    return middlewares\n```\n\nComments in the code explicitly document WHY each middleware must be in its position. This ordering discipline is a key design choice.\n\n### Pattern 1: DanglingToolCallMiddleware — Conversation Repair\n\n**Problem it solves:** When a conversation is interrupted (process crash, timeout, network error), the message history can end up with an AI message containing `tool_calls` but no corresponding `ToolMessage` responses. This breaks most LLM APIs, which expect tool calls to always have matching tool results.\n\n**How it works:** Wraps the model call to inspect the message history and inject synthetic error `ToolMessage`s for any unmatched tool calls:\n\n```python\nclass DanglingToolCallMiddleware(AgentMiddleware):\n    def wrap_model_call(self, request, handler):\n        messages = request.messages\n        patched = []\n        for msg in messages:\n            patched.append(msg)\n            if isinstance(msg, AIMessage) and msg.tool_calls:\n                # Check if next messages contain matching ToolMessages\n                existing_ids = set()\n                for next_msg in remaining_messages:\n                    if isinstance(next_msg, ToolMessage):\n                        existing_ids.add(next_msg.tool_call_id)\n                for tc in msg.tool_calls:\n                    if tc[\"id\"] not in existing_ids:\n                        patched.append(ToolMessage(\n                            content=\"[Tool call was interrupted and did not return a result.]\",\n                            tool_call_id=tc[\"id\"],\n                            name=tc.get(\"name\", \"unknown\"),\n                            status=\"error\",\n                        ))\n        return handler(request.override(messages=patched))\n```\n\n**Hermes equivalent:** Hermes has `_sanitize_tool_pairs()` in `context_compressor.py` that does something similar, but ONLY during context compression — not on every model call. This means dangling tool calls can still cause errors during normal operation if a session was interrupted.\n\n**What Hermes should adopt:** Run tool-call/tool-result integrity checking before EVERY model call, not just during compression. The context compressor's `_sanitize_tool_pairs()` logic could be lifted and applied at the pre-model-call stage.\n\n### Pattern 2: SubagentLimitMiddleware — Hard Enforcement via Message Rewriting\n\n**Problem it solves:** LLMs sometimes ignore prompt-based limits on concurrent subagent spawning. If the prompt says \"max 3 parallel tasks\" but the model emits 5 `task` tool calls, Hermes currently executes all 5.\n\n**How DeerFlow enforces it:** The middleware runs `after_model` and physically truncates excess tool calls from the AI message:\n\n```python\nclass SubagentLimitMiddleware(AgentMiddleware):\n    def _truncate_task_calls(self, state):\n        last_msg = state[\"messages\"][-1]\n        tool_calls = last_msg.tool_calls or []\n        task_indices = [i for i, tc in enumerate(tool_calls) if tc.get(\"name\") == \"task\"]\n        if len(task_indices) <= self.max_concurrent:\n            return None  # Within limit\n        # Truncate excess task calls\n        indices_to_drop = set(task_indices[self.max_concurrent:])\n        truncated = [tc for i, tc in enumerate(tool_calls) if i not in indices_to_drop]\n        updated_msg = last_msg.model_copy(update={\"tool_calls\": truncated})\n        return {\"messages\": [updated_msg]}  # Same ID = replacement\n```\n\n**Key technique:** Uses `model_copy(update=...)` to create a new message with the same ID, which LangGraph's state reducer treats as a **replacement** rather than an append. This is elegant — it doesn't break the message chain, just silently drops excess tool calls.\n\n**Hermes equivalent:** Hermes's `delegate_tool.py` has `MAX_CONCURRENT_CHILDREN = 3` enforced in the batch execution code, but this only limits the batch mode (explicit array of tasks). When the LLM makes multiple independent `delegate_task` calls across turns, there's no aggregate limit. And there's no mechanism to rewrite the AI's tool calls before execution.\n\n**What Hermes should adopt:** Post-model-call interception that can inspect and modify tool calls before dispatch. This enables:\n- Hard subagent concurrency limits\n- Tool call deduplication (prevent identical calls in same turn)\n- Tool call filtering (block certain tools based on runtime state)\n- Cost/rate limit enforcement\n\n### Pattern 3: ClarificationMiddleware — Clean Execution Interrupts\n\n**How it works:** Wraps tool calls and intercepts `ask_clarification` specifically. Instead of executing it as a normal tool, it returns a `Command(goto=END)` that halts the agent loop and surfaces the question to the user:\n\n```python\ndef wrap_tool_call(self, request, handler):\n    if request.tool_call.get(\"name\") != \"ask_clarification\":\n        return handler(request)\n    # Format and present the clarification question\n    return Command(update={\"messages\": [tool_message]}, goto=END)\n```\n\n**Hermes equivalent:** Hermes's `clarify` tool works differently — it's a normal tool that returns the user's response. The agent loop continues after the user responds. DeerFlow's approach is more like a hard interrupt.\n\n### Other Middlewares Worth Noting\n\n- **ViewImageMiddleware**: Injects base64 image data as multimodal content blocks before model calls when images were viewed. Only activates for vision-capable models.\n- **MemoryMiddleware**: Queues conversation for async memory update after agent finishes (ties into the debounced memory queue from #625).\n- **UploadsMiddleware**: Scans uploaded files and injects `<uploaded_files>` XML block into the last human message. Validates filenames (rejects path traversal).\n\n---\n\n## Current State in Hermes Agent\n\nHermes has several \"middleware-like\" patterns scattered across the codebase:\n\n| Pattern | Location | Type |\n|---------|----------|------|\n| Hook system | `gateway/hooks.py` | Event-driven, post-hoc |\n| Dangerous command approval | `tools/approval.py` | Tool-specific interception |\n| Memory nudge injection | `gateway/run.py` | Ad-hoc message modification |\n| Content scanning | `tools/memory_tool.py` | Input validation |\n| Tool-call/result sanitization | `agent/context_compressor.py` | Compression-time only |\n| System prompt layering | `agent/prompt_builder.py` | Build-time composition |\n| Auto-image analysis | `gateway/run.py` | Platform-specific pre-processing |\n| Iteration budget checking | `run_agent.py` | Loop-level check |\n\nThese work but are:\n1. **Scattered** — no single place to see all interceptions\n2. **Inconsistent** — different mechanisms for different interceptions\n3. **Incomplete** — no pre-model-call or post-model-call hooks (only tool-level)\n4. **Not composable** — can't easily add new interceptions without touching core code\n\n---\n\n## Implementation Plan\n\n### Classification\n\nThis is a **core architecture change** to `run_agent.py` and potentially `model_tools.py`. Not a skill or tool.\n\n### What We'd Need\n\n1. Middleware base class with lifecycle hooks\n2. Middleware chain builder with explicit ordering\n3. Migration of existing scattered patterns into middlewares\n4. Pre-model-call and post-model-call hook points in the agent loop\n\n### Phased Rollout\n\n**Phase 1: Pre-model-call tool-pair sanitization (immediate win, low risk)**\n- Lift `_sanitize_tool_pairs()` from `context_compressor.py` and apply it before every LLM call in `run_agent.py`\n- This alone fixes the dangling tool call problem without a full middleware architecture\n- Minimal code change, high reliability improvement\n\n**Phase 2: Post-model-call tool-call interception (subagent limits + dedup)**\n- Add a hook point after the model responds but before tool calls are dispatched\n- Implement hard subagent limit enforcement (inspect tool_calls, truncate excess `delegate_task` calls)\n- Implement tool call deduplication (same tool + same args in one turn → deduplicate)\n- Wire into existing `MAX_CONCURRENT_CHILDREN` config\n\n**Phase 3: Formal middleware architecture**\n- Define `AgentMiddleware` base class with hooks: `before_turn`, `after_turn`, `pre_model`, `post_model`, `wrap_tool`\n- Ordered middleware chain with documented ordering constraints\n- Migrate existing patterns:\n  - Memory nudge → middleware\n  - Dangerous command approval → `wrap_tool` middleware\n  - Iteration budget → `pre_model` middleware\n  - Auto-image analysis → `pre_model` middleware\n- Extension point: user-defined middlewares loaded from config/hooks directory\n\n---\n\n## Pros & Cons\n\n### Pros\n- **Phase 1 alone is a bug fix** — dangling tool calls cause real API errors today\n- **Hard enforcement** beats prompt-only soft limits for subagent concurrency\n- **Consolidates** scattered interception patterns into a single, auditable chain\n- **Composable** — new behaviors can be added as middlewares without touching core loop\n- **Testable** — each middleware can be unit tested in isolation\n\n### Cons / Risks\n- Full middleware architecture (Phase 3) is a significant refactor\n- Ordering constraints add cognitive overhead for contributors\n- Performance overhead of running middleware chain on every model call (minimal — these are fast checks)\n- Risk of over-engineering if only 2-3 middlewares ever exist\n\n---\n\n## Open Questions\n\n- Should Phase 1 (dangling tool call repair) be implemented immediately as a standalone fix, independent of the middleware architecture?\n- Should the middleware system support async hooks, or is sync sufficient?\n- How should middleware state be scoped? (Per-turn vs. per-session vs. global)\n- Should user-defined middlewares be supported, or only built-in ones?\n- Does the tool call truncation approach (rewriting AI messages) work correctly with all LLM providers' conversation format?\n\n---\n\n## References\n\n- [DeerFlow middleware implementations](https://github.com/bytedance/deer-flow/tree/main/backend/src/agents/middlewares)\n- [DeerFlow DanglingToolCallMiddleware](https://github.com/bytedance/deer-flow/blob/main/backend/src/agents/middlewares/dangling_tool_call_middleware.py)\n- [DeerFlow SubagentLimitMiddleware](https://github.com/bytedance/deer-flow/blob/main/backend/src/agents/middlewares/subagent_limit_middleware.py)\n- [DeerFlow ClarificationMiddleware](https://github.com/bytedance/deer-flow/blob/main/backend/src/agents/middlewares/clarification_middleware.py)\n- [DeerFlow lead agent middleware ordering](https://github.com/bytedance/deer-flow/blob/main/backend/src/agents/lead_agent/agent.py)\n- Hermes Agent: `run_agent.py`, `agent/context_compressor.py`, `gateway/hooks.py`, `tools/approval.py`\n- Related: #520 (Tool Call Repair), #359 (Extension System), #512 (Doom Loop Detection), #481 (Loop Guard)\n","labels":[{"id":"LA_kwDOPRF1G88AAAACF7GHow","name":"enhancement","description":"New feature or request","color":"a2eeef"}],"title":"Feature: Agent Loop Middleware Architecture — Ordered Lifecycle Hooks, Hard Subagent Limit Enforcement & Dangling Tool Call Repair (inspired by DeerFlow)"}
```

> AGENT

Here is a summary of all relevant issues found in the NousResearch/hermes-agent repository.

## Results

After extensive searching using all the specified queries plus additional terms (delegate_task, SessionDB, sqlite, race condition, deadlock, parallel, lock, crash, subagent, thread safe, concurrent), **no issues were found that directly report crashes/segfaults, "database is locked" errors, redirect_stdout threading issues, or thread safety bugs** in the traditional sense. The repository has no filed bug reports about segfaults, sqlite locking contention, or redirect_stdout problems.

However, the following issues are tangentially relevant to the topics you asked about:

### Concurrency / Threading / Parallel Subagent Issues

- **#1468** (OPEN) -- "Feature Request: Parallel Task Execution for Concurrent User Requests" -- Requests the ability to handle multiple user requests concurrently. Notes that currently the system either interrupts the running task or queues follow-ups; no true parallelism exists.

- **#814** (OPEN) -- "Group/channel sessions shared across all users -- context bloats fast with multiple active users" -- Multiple users in a Discord/Telegram channel share one session. Notes that the `_running_agents` / `_pending_messages` interrupt mechanism means concurrent messages from different users interfere with each other.

- **#1182** (OPEN) -- "bug: setup flow writes provider to config.yaml before model selection, causing gateway race condition" -- A race condition where the gateway reads config mid-update, causing it to send requests with an incompatible model name to the wrong provider.

- **#626** (OPEN, enhancement) -- "Agent Loop Middleware Architecture -- Ordered Lifecycle Hooks, Hard Subagent Limit Enforcement & Dangling Tool Call Repair" -- Documents that there is no hard enforcement of subagent concurrency limits (only a soft `MAX_CONCURRENT_CHILDREN = 3` in batch mode), and no post-model-call interception to truncate excess delegate_task calls.

### SQLite / SessionDB Issues

- **#860** (CLOSED) -- "bug: SQLite session transcript accumulates duplicate messages (3-4x token inflation)" -- Three separate code paths all write to the same SQLite `state.db` with no deduplication, causing 3-4x message inflation. Two separate `SessionDB()` instances connect to the same file. This is the closest issue to a "database" concurrency problem, though it manifests as data duplication rather than locking errors.

- **#105** (CLOSED) -- "Gateway doesn't pass session_db to AIAgent, causing 'session_search must be handled by the agent loop' error" -- The gateway was not initializing/passing the SessionDB to the agent, so session_search always failed in gateway mode.

### Delegation Issues

- **#1011** (CLOSED, bug) -- "[Bug]: agent delegation to custom inference provider does not work" -- Delegated tasks ignore the custom provider specified in config and always use the main model. Not a crash, but a delegation configuration bug.

### Session / Context Issues (related to concurrent usage)

- **#1056** (OPEN, bug) -- "[Bug]: Telegram and Discord DMs share a single global session (Context Bleeding)" -- All DM users on Telegram/Discord share one session key, causing cross-user context bleeding. A privacy and correctness issue stemming from how session keys are constructed.

- **#886** (OPEN) -- "Context compression silently drops history due to Responses API / stream kwarg mismatch in auxiliary client" -- Context compression fails silently 100% of the time with certain configurations, dropping conversation history without summarizing it.

### Key Takeaway

The repository does **not** have any filed issues about:
- Segfaults or crashes when running concurrent subagents / delegate_task
- "database is locked" SQLite errors
- redirect_stdout threading problems
- General thread safety bugs in the agent core

These problems may exist in the codebase but have not been reported as GitHub issues. The closest related issues are #860 (duplicate SQLite writes from multiple code paths), #814/#1056 (shared session interference from concurrent users), and #626 (lack of hard subagent concurrency enforcement).

```
1→"""
     2→Local-only HTTP control API for the Hermes gateway.
     3→
     4→Runs alongside the gateway as a background aiohttp server on 127.0.0.1.
     5→Provides programmatic control over live agent sessions — session listing,
     6→message injection, etc.
     7→
     8→External tools (e.g., desloppify) can call these endpoints to influence
     9→a running Hermes agent without needing direct process access.
    10→
    11→All mutations go through POST /sessions/{key}/message — inject any text
    12→(including /commands) as if the user typed it.
    13→
    14→Default port: 47823 (override with HERMES_CONTROL_PORT env var).
    15→"""
    16→
    17→import json
    18→import logging
    19→import os
    20→
    21→from aiohttp import web
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→DEFAULT_PORT = 47823
    26→
    27→# Commands available via /message injection.  This list is informational —
    28→# the message endpoint accepts any text, but GET /commands exposes these
    29→# so external tools know what's available.

    32→    {"command": "/new", "description": "Start a new conversation"},
    33→    {"command": "/stop", "description": "Stop the running agent"},
    34→    {"command": "/compact", "description": "Compress conversation context"},
    35→    {"command": "/compress", "description": "Compress conversation context"},
    36→    {"command": "/status", "description": "Show session info"},
    37→    {"command": "/model <provider:model>", "description": "Switch model"},
    38→    {"command": "/personality <name>", "description": "Switch personality"},
    39→    {"command": "/autoreply <instructions>", "description": "Enable auto-reply loop"},
    40→    {"command": "/autoreply off", "description": "Disable auto-reply loop"},
    41→    {"command": "/reasoning <level>", "description": "Set reasoning effort"},
    42→    {"command": "/rollback [number]", "description": "List or restore checkpoints"},
    43→    {"command": "/background <prompt>", "description": "Run prompt in background session"},
    44→    {"command": "/undo", "description": "Undo last assistant response"},
    45→    {"command": "/retry", "description": "Retry last message"},
    46→    {"command": "/usage", "description": "Show token usage"},
    47→    {"command": "/help", "description": "List commands"},
    48→]
    49→
    50→
    51→def _get_port() -> int:
    52→    return int(os.getenv("HERMES_CONTROL_PORT", str(DEFAULT_PORT)))
    53→
    54→
    55→def _json_response(data: dict, status: int = 200) -> web.Response:
    56→    return web.Response(
    57→        text=json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False),
    58→        content_type="application/json",
    59→        status=status,
    60→    )

    62→
    63→class ControlAPI:
    64→    """Lightweight control API that holds a reference to the GatewayRunner."""
    65→
    66→    def __init__(self, gateway_runner):
    67→        self.runner = gateway_runner
    68→        self.app = web.Application()
    69→        self._setup_routes()
    70→        self._site = None
    71→
    72→    def _setup_routes(self):
    73→        self.app.router.add_get("/health", self.health)
    74→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions", self.list_sessions)
    75→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions/{key}", self.get_session)
    76→        self.app.router.add_get("/commands", self.list_commands)
    77→        self.app.router.add_post("/sessions/{key}/message", self.send_message)
    78→
    79→    # ── Helpers ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    80→
    81→    def _resolve_agent(self, key: str):
    82→        """Look up a running agent by session key.  '_any' returns the first.
    83→        Returns (resolved_key, agent) or (key, None) if not found."""
    84→        if key == "_any":
    85→            if not self.runner._running_agents:
    86→                return key, None
    87→            key = next(iter(self.runner._running_agents))
    88→        return key, self.runner._running_agents.get(key)
    89→
    90→    # ── Endpoints ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    91→
    92→    async def health(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    93→        return _json_response({"status": "ok"})
    94→
    95→    async def list_sessions(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    96→        """List all sessions, marking which have a live (running) agent."""
    97→        sessions = []
    98→        for key, agent in self.runner._running_agents.items():
    99→            entry = {
   100→                "session_key": key,
   101→                "status": "running",
   102→                "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
   103→                "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
   104→                **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   105→            }
   106→            sessions.append(entry)
   107→        return _json_response({"sessions": sessions})

   110→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   111→        key, agent = self._resolve_agent(key)
   112→        if not agent:
   113→            return _json_response(
   114→                {"error": f"No running agent for '{key}'"},
   115→                status=404,
   116→            )
   117→        return _json_response({
   118→            "session_key": key,
   119→            "status": "running",
   120→            "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),

   122→            **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   123→        })
   124→
   125→    async def list_commands(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   126→        """List available slash commands that can be sent via /message."""
   127→        return _json_response({"commands": AVAILABLE_COMMANDS})
   128→
   129→    async def send_message(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   130→        """Inject a message (or /command) into a session.
   131→
   132→        POST body: {"text": "...", "mode": "interrupt"|"queue"}
   133→
   134→        Modes:
   135→        - interrupt (default): cancel current agent run, process this message
   136→        - queue: let current run finish, then process this message next
   137→        """
   138→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   139→
   140→        try:
   141→            body = await request.json()
   142→        except Exception:
   143→            return _json_response({"error": "Invalid JSON body"}, status=400)
   144→
   145→        text = body.get("text", "").strip()
   146→        if not text:
   147→            return _json_response({"error": "'text' is required"}, status=400)
   148→
   149→        mode = body.get("mode", "interrupt")
   150→        if mode not in ("interrupt", "queue"):
   151→            return _json_response(
   152→                {"error": f"Invalid mode '{mode}', must be 'interrupt' or 'queue'"},
   153→                status=400,
   154→            )
   155→
   156→        logger.info(
   157→            "Control API: send_message session=%s mode=%s text=%s",
   158→            key, mode, text[:80],
   159→        )
   160→
   161→        try:
   162→            result = self.runner.inject_message(key, text, interrupt=(mode != "queue"))
   163→            # await if coroutine (GatewayRunner is async, CLI shim is sync)
   164→            if hasattr(result, "__await__"):
   165→                await result
   166→        except LookupError as e:
   167→            return _json_response({"error": str(e)}, status=404)
   168→
   169→        return _json_response({"success": True, "message": "Message submitted"})
   170→
   171→    # ── Lifecycle ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   172→
   173→    async def start(self):
   174→        port = _get_port()
   175→        app_runner = web.AppRunner(self.app, access_log=None)
   176→        await app_runner.setup()
   177→        self._site = web.TCPSite(app_runner, "127.0.0.1", port)
   178→        try:
   179→            await self._site.start()
   180→            logger.info("Control API listening on http://127.0.0.1:%d", port)
   181→            self._write_port_file(port)
   182→        except OSError as e:
   183→            logger.warning("Control API failed to bind port %d: %s", port, e)
   184→
   185→    async def stop(self):
   186→        if self._site:
   187→            await self._site.stop()
   188→            self._remove_port_file()
   189→            logger.info("Control API stopped")
   190→
   191→    @staticmethod
   192→    def _write_port_file(port: int):
   193→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   194→        try:

   203→        try:
```

```
1→"""
     2→Hermes control client — lightweight module for external tools to control
     3→a running Hermes instance (CLI or gateway).
     4→
     5→Usage:
     6→    from gateway.hermes_control_client import HermesControl
     7→
     8→    ctl = HermesControl()  # auto-discovers port from ~/.hermes/control_api.port
     9→
    10→    # Send a message (interrupt mode — stops current work)
    11→    ctl.send_message("Focus on this instead")
    12→
    13→    # Queue a message (processed after current work finishes)
    14→    ctl.send_message("When you're done, also check logs", mode="queue")
    15→
    16→    # Send a slash command
    17→    ctl.send_message("/reset")
    18→    ctl.send_message("/model openrouter:anthropic/claude-sonnet-4")
    19→
    20→    # List running sessions
    21→    sessions = ctl.list_sessions()
    22→
    23→    # List available commands
    24→    commands = ctl.list_commands()
    25→
    26→Zero dependencies beyond stdlib (uses urllib, not requests/aiohttp).
    27→"""
    28→
    29→import json
    30→import os
    31→import urllib.request
    32→import urllib.error
    33→from typing import Optional
    34→
    35→DEFAULT_PORT = 47823
    36→PORT_FILE = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
    37→
    38→
    39→class HermesControl:
    40→    """Client for the Hermes control API."""
    41→
    42→    def __init__(self, host: str = "127.0.0.1", port: Optional[int] = None):
    43→        if port is None:
    44→            port = self._discover_port()
    45→        self.base_url = f"http://{host}:{port}"
    46→
    47→    @staticmethod
    48→    def _discover_port() -> int:
    49→        try:
    50→            with open(PORT_FILE) as f:
    51→                return int(f.read().strip())
    52→        except (OSError, ValueError):
    53→            return DEFAULT_PORT
    54→
    55→    def _request(self, method: str, path: str, body: dict = None) -> dict:
    56→        url = f"{self.base_url}{path}"
    57→        data = json.dumps(body).encode() if body else None
    58→        req = urllib.request.Request(
    59→            url,
    60→            data=data,
    61→            method=method,
    62→            headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"} if data else {},
    63→        )
    64→        try:
    65→            with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=5) as resp:
    66→                return json.loads(resp.read())
    67→        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
    68→            return json.loads(e.read())
    69→        except urllib.error.URLError as e:
    70→            return {"error": f"Cannot reach Hermes control API: {e.reason}"}
    71→
    72→    # ── Read-only endpoints ────────────────────────────────────────────
    73→
    74→    def health(self) -> dict:
    75→        """Check if the control API is running."""
    76→        return self._request("GET", "/health")
    77→
    78→    def list_sessions(self) -> dict:
    79→        """List all running agent sessions."""
    80→        return self._request("GET", "/sessions")
    81→
    82→    def get_session(self, session_key: str = "_any") -> dict:
    83→        """Get info about a specific session."""
    84→        return self._request("GET", f"/sessions/{session_key}")
    85→
    86→    def list_commands(self) -> dict:
    87→        """List available slash commands."""
    88→        return self._request("GET", "/commands")
    89→
    90→    # ── Message injection ──────────────────────────────────────────────
    91→
    92→    def send_message(
    93→        self,
    94→        text: str,
    95→        session_key: str = "_any",
    96→        mode: str = "interrupt",
    97→    ) -> dict:
    98→        """Send a message (or /command) into a running session.
    99→
   100→        Args:
   101→            text: Message text or slash command (e.g., "hello", "/reset").
   102→            session_key: Session to target. "_any" targets the first running session.
   103→            mode: "interrupt" (default) stops current work; "queue" waits for it to finish.
   104→        """
   105→        return self._request("POST", f"/sessions/{session_key}/message", {
   106→            "text": text,
   107→            "mode": mode,
   108→        })
   109→
   110→    # ── Convenience methods ────────────────────────────────────────────
   111→
   112→    def switch_model(self, provider: str, model: str, **kw) -> dict:
   113→        """Switch model via /model command."""
   114→        return self.send_message(f"/model {provider}:{model}", **kw)
   115→
   116→    def compact_context(self, **kw) -> dict:
   117→        """Trigger context compaction."""
   118→        return self.send_message("/compact", **kw)
   119→
   120→    def reset(self, **kw) -> dict:
   121→        """Reset conversation history."""
   122→        return self.send_message("/reset", **kw)
   123→
   124→    def stop(self, **kw) -> dict:
   125→        """Stop the running agent."""
   126→        return self.send_message("/stop", **kw)
   127→
   128→    def is_autoreply_enabled(self, session_key: str = "_any") -> bool:
   129→        """Check if autoreply is enabled for a session."""
   130→        info = self.get_session(session_key)
   131→        return bool(info.get("autoreply", {}).get("enabled"))
   132→
   133→    def is_available(self) -> bool:
   134→        """Check if a Hermes instance with control API is reachable."""
   135→        try:
   136→            return self.health().get("status") == "ok"
   137→        except Exception:
   138→            return False
   139→
```

```
+    def get_session_info(self, key: str) -> dict:
+        """Return session state for the control API."""

+    async def inject_message(self, key: str, text: str, *, interrupt: bool = True):
+        """Inject a synthetic message into a session via its platform adapter.
+
+        Resolves the session key ('_any' picks the first running session),
+        builds a MessageEvent from the session's stored origin, and hands it
+        to the adapter's handle_message with the requested interrupt mode.
+        """

+                raise LookupError("No running session to inject into")
+            key = next(iter(self._running_agents))

+        self.session_store._ensure_loaded()
+        entry = self.session_store._entries.get(key)
+        if not entry or not entry.origin:
+            raise LookupError(f"No session found for '{key}'")
+
+        source = entry.origin
+        adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
+        if not adapter:
+            raise LookupError(f"No adapter for platform '{source.platform}'")
+
+        event = MessageEvent(
+            text=text,
+            source=source,
+            message_id=f"control-{time.time()}",
+        )
+        await adapter.handle_message(event, interrupt=interrupt)
```

```
1→"""Tests for the control API."""
     2→
     3→import json
     4→from unittest.mock import AsyncMock, MagicMock
     5→
     6→import pytest
     7→
     8→from gateway.control_api import ControlAPI
     9→
    10→
    11→# ── Helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    12→
    13→
    14→def _make_control_api():
    15→    """Create a ControlAPI with a mock runner."""
    16→    runner = MagicMock()
    17→    runner._running_agents = {"sess1": MagicMock(model="test-model", provider="test")}
    18→    runner.inject_message = MagicMock(return_value=None)
    19→    api = ControlAPI(runner)
    20→    return api, runner
    21→
    22→
    23→def _make_request(key, body=None):
    24→    request = MagicMock()
    25→    request.match_info = {"key": key}
    26→    if body is not None:
    27→        request.json = AsyncMock(return_value=body)
    28→    return request
    29→
    30→
    31→# ── send_message tests ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
    32→
    33→
    34→@pytest.mark.asyncio
    35→async def test_send_message_calls_inject_message():
    36→    """POST /message delegates to runner.inject_message."""
    37→    api, runner = _make_control_api()
    38→    request = _make_request("sess1", {"text": "hello", "mode": "interrupt"})
    39→
    40→    resp = await api.send_message(request)
    41→
    42→    assert resp.status == 200
    43→    runner.inject_message.assert_called_once_with("sess1", "hello", interrupt=True)
    44→
    45→
    46→@pytest.mark.asyncio
    47→async def test_send_message_queue_mode():
    48→    """Queue mode passes interrupt=False."""
    49→    api, runner = _make_control_api()
    50→    request = _make_request("sess1", {"text": "/compact", "mode": "queue"})
    51→
    52→    resp = await api.send_message(request)
    53→
    54→    assert resp.status == 200
    55→    runner.inject_message.assert_called_once_with("sess1", "/compact", interrupt=False)
    56→
    57→
    58→@pytest.mark.asyncio
    59→async def test_send_message_defaults_to_interrupt():
    60→    """Mode defaults to interrupt when not specified."""
    61→    api, runner = _make_control_api()
    62→    request = _make_request("_any", {"text": "stop"})
    63→
    64→    resp = await api.send_message(request)
    65→
    66→    assert resp.status == 200
    67→    runner.inject_message.assert_called_once_with("_any", "stop", interrupt=True)
    68→
    69→
    70→@pytest.mark.asyncio
    71→async def test_send_message_empty_text_returns_400():
    72→    """Empty text should return 400."""
    73→    api, runner = _make_control_api()
    74→    request = _make_request("sess1", {"text": "  "})
    75→
    76→    resp = await api.send_message(request)
    77→
    78→    assert resp.status == 400
    79→    runner.inject_message.assert_not_called()
    80→
    81→
    82→@pytest.mark.asyncio
    83→async def test_send_message_invalid_mode_returns_400():
    84→    """Invalid mode should return 400."""
    85→    api, runner = _make_control_api()

    88→    resp = await api.send_message(request)

    90→    assert resp.status == 400

    93→
    94→@pytest.mark.asyncio
```

```
1→"""
     2→Gateway subcommand for hermes CLI.
     3→
     4→Handles: hermes gateway [run|start|stop|restart|status|install|uninstall|setup]
     5→"""
     6→
     7→import asyncio
     8→import os
     9→import signal
    10→import subprocess
    11→import sys
    12→from pathlib import Path
    13→
    14→PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).parent.parent.resolve()
    15→
    16→from hermes_cli.config import get_env_value, save_env_value
    17→from hermes_cli.setup import (
    18→    print_header, print_info, print_success, print_warning, print_error,
    19→    prompt, prompt_choice, prompt_yes_no,
    20→)
    21→from hermes_cli.colors import Colors, color
    22→
    23→
    24→# =============================================================================
    25→# Process Management (for manual gateway runs)
    26→# =============================================================================
    27→
    28→def find_gateway_pids() -> list:
    29→    """Find PIDs of running gateway processes."""
    30→    pids = []
    31→    patterns = [
    32→        "hermes_cli.main gateway",
    33→        "hermes gateway",
    34→        "gateway/run.py",
    35→    ]
    36→
    37→    try:
    38→        if is_windows():
    39→            # Windows: use wmic to search command lines
    40→            result = subprocess.run(
    41→                ["wmic", "process", "get", "ProcessId,CommandLine", "/FORMAT:LIST"],
    42→                capture_output=True, text=True
    43→            )
    44→            # Parse WMIC LIST output: blocks of "CommandLine=...\nProcessId=...\n"
    45→            current_cmd = ""
    46→            for line in result.stdout.split('\n'):
    47→                line = line.strip()
    48→                if line.startswith("CommandLine="):
    49→                    current_cmd = line[len("CommandLine="):]
    50→                elif line.startswith("ProcessId="):
    51→                    pid_str = line[len("ProcessId="):]
    52→                    if any(p in current_cmd for p in patterns):
    53→                        try:
    54→                            pid = int(pid_str)
    55→                            if pid != os.getpid() and pid not in pids:
    56→                                pids.append(pid)
    57→                        except ValueError:
    58→                            pass
    59→                    current_cmd = ""
    60→        else:
    61→            result = subprocess.run(
    62→                ["ps", "aux"],
    63→                capture_output=True,
    64→                text=True
    65→            )
    66→            for line in result.stdout.split('\n'):
    67→                # Skip grep and current process
    68→                if 'grep' in line or str(os.getpid()) in line:
    69→                    continue
    70→                for pattern in patterns:
    71→                    if pattern in line:
    72→                        parts = line.split()
    73→                        if len(parts) > 1:
    74→                            try:
    75→                                pid = int(parts[1])
    76→                                if pid not in pids:
    77→                                    pids.append(pid)
    78→                            except ValueError:
    79→                                continue
    80→                        break
    81→    except Exception:
    82→        pass
    83→
    84→    return pids
    85→
    86→
    87→def kill_gateway_processes(force: bool = False) -> int:
    88→    """Kill any running gateway processes. Returns count killed."""
    89→    pids = find_gateway_pids()
    90→    killed = 0
    91→    
    92→    for pid in pids:
    93→        try:
    94→            if force and not is_windows():
    95→                os.kill(pid, signal.SIGKILL)
    96→            else:
    97→                os.kill(pid, signal.SIGTERM)
    98→            killed += 1
    99→        except ProcessLookupError:
   100→            # Process already gone
```

```
1→"""Auto-reply engine — shared logic for CLI and gateway.
     2→
     3→Handles config parsing, state management, prompt building, and LLM calls.
     4→Consumers (CLI, gateway) only handle injection into their respective message loops.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
     8→
     9→_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS = 20
    10→
    11→
    12→def parse_autoreply_args(args: str) -> Tuple[str, Optional[Dict[str, Any]]]:
    13→    """Parse /autoreply command arguments.
    14→
    15→    Returns (status_message, config_or_none).
    16→    - When enabling: returns (message, new_config_dict)
    17→    - When disabling/status/error: returns (message, None)
    18→
    19→    The caller is responsible for storing/clearing the config and
    20→    formatting the message for their platform (markdown vs plain text).
    21→    """
    22→    # /autoreply off
    23→    if args.lower() in ("off", "disable", "stop"):
    24→        return "off", None
    25→
    26→    # /autoreply max <N>
    27→    if args.lower() == "max" or (args.lower().startswith("max ") and len(args) > 4):
    28→        parts = args.split(None, 1)
    29→        if len(parts) == 2:
    30→            try:
    31→                n = int(parts[1])
    32→                if n < 1:
    33→                    return "error:Max turns must be at least 1.", None
    34→                return f"max:{n}", None
    35→            except ValueError:
    36→                return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    37→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    38→
    39→    # /autoreply (no args)
    40→    if not args:
    41→        return "status", None
    42→
    43→    # Extract flags from args: --literal, --max N, --forever
    44→    literal = False
    45→    max_turns = _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS
    46→    remaining_parts = []
    47→
    48→    parts = args.split()
    49→    i = 0
    50→    while i < len(parts):
    51→        if parts[i] == "--literal":
    52→            literal = True
    53→        elif parts[i] == "--forever":
    54→            max_turns = 0  # 0 = unlimited
    55→        elif parts[i] == "--max" and i + 1 < len(parts):
    56→            i += 1
    57→            try:
    58→                n = int(parts[i])
    59→                if n < 1:
    60→                    return "error:--max must be at least 1.", None
    61→                max_turns = n
    62→            except ValueError:
    63→                return "error:--max requires a number.", None
    64→        else:
    65→            remaining_parts.append(parts[i])
    66→        i += 1
    67→
    68→    prompt = " ".join(remaining_parts).strip()
    69→
    70→    if literal:
    71→        if not prompt:
    72→            return "error:Usage: /autoreply --literal <message>", None
    73→        return "enabled", {
    74→            "prompt": prompt,
    75→            "model": None,
    76→            "max_turns": max_turns,
    77→            "turn_count": 0,
    78→            "literal": True,
    79→        }
    80→
    81→    if not prompt:
    82→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply <instructions>", None
    83→
    84→    return "enabled", {
    85→        "prompt": prompt,
    86→        "model": None,
    87→        "max_turns": max_turns,
    88→        "turn_count": 0,
    89→    }
    90→
    91→
    92→def build_autoreply_messages(config: Dict[str, Any],
    93→                             history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    94→    """Build the message list for the auto-reply LLM call.
    95→
    96→    Filters history to recent user/assistant messages, prepends a system
    97→    prompt with the user's instructions, and appends the generation request.
    98→    """
    99→    recent = [
   100→        m for m in (history or [])
   101→        if m.get("role") in ("user", "assistant") and m.get("content")
   102→    ][-20:]
   103→
   104→    system_prompt = (
   105→        "You are generating a reply on behalf of the user in a conversation "
   106→        "with an AI assistant.\n\n"
   107→        "THE USER'S INSTRUCTIONS — follow these as your top priority:\n"
   108→        f"{config['prompt']}\n\n"
   109→        "Output ONLY the user's reply message. No labels, no meta-commentary, "
   110→        "no 'User:' prefix."
   111→    )
   112→
   113→    messages = [{"role": "system", "content": system_prompt}]
   114→    for m in recent:
   115→        content = m["content"]
   116→        # Flatten list-type content to plain text
   117→        if isinstance(content, list):
   118→            content = "\n".join(
   119→                p.get("text", "") for p in content
   120→                if isinstance(p, dict) and p.get("type") == "text"
   121→            )
   122→        messages.append({"role": m["role"], "content": content})
   123→    messages.append({
   124→        "role": "user",
   125→        "content": "Based on the conversation above and your instructions, "
   126→                   "generate the next user reply.",
   127→    })
   128→    return messages
   129→
   130→
   131→def check_and_advance(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> Tuple[Optional[str], bool]:
   132→    """Check turn cap and handle literal mode.
   133→
   134→    Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).
   135→    - (None, False): config is None / not applicable (caller didn't pass config)
   136→    - (None, True): turn cap reached, config should be removed
   137→    - (text, False): literal mode reply, turn_count incremented
   138→    - None for LLM mode: caller should proceed to call the LLM
   139→
   140→    For LLM mode, returns a sentinel to indicate the caller should proceed:
   141→    the return is (None, False) when turn_count < max_turns and not literal.
   142→    But we can't distinguish "no config" from "LLM needed" with just (None, False).
   143→
   144→    So the contract is: caller checks config existence first, then calls this.
   145→    If this returns (None, True) → cap reached. If it returns a string → use it.
   146→    If it returns (None, False) → proceed to LLM call.
   147→    """
   148→    if config["max_turns"] > 0 and config["turn_count"] >= config["max_turns"]:
   149→        return None, True
   150→
   151→    if config.get("literal"):
   152→        config["turn_count"] += 1
   153→        return config["prompt"], False
   154→
   155→    return None, False
   156→
   157→
   158→def format_status(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
   159→    """Format the auto-reply status for display."""
   160→    mode = "literal" if config.get("literal") else "LLM-generated"
   161→    label = "Message" if config.get("literal") else "Prompt"
   162→    prompt_preview = config["prompt"][:100]
   163→    if len(config["prompt"]) > 100:
   164→        prompt_preview += "..."
   165→    return (
   166→        f"Auto-reply active ({mode})\n"
   167→        f"  {label}: {prompt_preview}\n"
   168→        f"  Turns: {config['turn_count']}/{'∞' if config['max_turns'] == 0 else config['max_turns']}"
   169→    )
   170→
```

```
    51→    mock_store.get_or_create_session.return_value = MagicMock(
    52→        session_id="sess_123",
    53→        session_key="agent:main:telegram:dm:12345",
    54→        created_at=MagicMock(strftime=lambda f: "2026-01-01 00:00"),
    55→        updated_at=MagicMock(strftime=lambda f: "2026-01-01 00:00"),
    56→        total_tokens=0,
    57→        last_prompt_tokens=0,
    58→        was_auto_reset=False,
    59→    )
    60→    mock_store._generate_session_key.return_value = "agent:main:telegram:dm:12345"
    61→    mock_store.load_transcript.return_value = []
    62→    runner.session_store = mock_store
    63→
    64→    from gateway.hooks import HookRegistry
    65→    runner.hooks = HookRegistry()
    66→
    67→    return runner
    68→

   246→
   247→class TestGenerateAutoreply:
   248→    """Tests for GatewayRunner._generate_autoreply."""
   249→
   250→    @pytest.mark.asyncio
   251→    async def test_returns_none_when_not_configured(self):
   252→        runner = _make_runner()
   253→        text, cap = await runner._generate_autoreply("no_such_key", "sess_123")
   254→        assert text is None

   257→    @pytest.mark.asyncio

   262→        }

   267→
   268→    @pytest.mark.asyncio
   269→    async def test_literal_mode_skips_llm(self):
   270→        """Literal mode returns the prompt directly without calling the LLM."""
   271→        runner = _make_runner()
   272→        runner._autoreply_configs["key"] = {
   273→            "prompt": "continue", "model": None,
   274→            "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, "turn_count": 0, "literal": True,
   275→        }
   276→        # No LLM mock needed — literal mode shouldn't call it
   277→        text, cap = await runner._generate_autoreply("key", "sess_123")
   278→        assert text == "continue"
   279→        assert cap is False
   280→        assert runner._autoreply_configs["key"]["turn_count"] == 1
   281→
   282→    @pytest.mark.asyncio
   283→    async def test_generates_reply_and_increments_counter(self):
   284→        runner = _make_runner()
   285→        runner._autoreply_configs["key"] = {
   286→            "prompt": "Ask follow-up questions",
   287→            "model": None,
   288→            "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS,
   289→            "turn_count": 2,
   290→        }
   291→        runner.session_store.load_transcript.return_value = [
   292→            {"role": "user", "content": "Tell me about Python"},
   293→            {"role": "assistant", "content": "Python is a programming language."},
   294→        ]
   295→
   296→        mock_response = MagicMock()
   297→        mock_response.choices = [MagicMock()]
   298→        mock_response.choices[0].message.content = "What about its type system?"
   299→
   300→        with patch("agent.auxiliary_client.async_call_llm", return_value=mock_response) as mock_llm:
   301→            text, cap = await runner._generate_autoreply("key", "sess_123")
   302→
   303→        assert text == "What about its type system?"
   304→        assert cap is False
   305→        assert runner._autoreply_configs["key"]["turn_count"] == 3
   306→
   307→        # Verify the LLM was called with task="autoreply"
   308→        call_kwargs = mock_llm.call_args[1]
   309→        assert call_kwargs["task"] == "autoreply"
   310→        assert call_kwargs["temperature"] == 0.7
   311→        # System prompt should contain the user's instructions
   312→        system_msg = call_kwargs["messages"][0]
   313→        assert system_msg["role"] == "system"
   314→        assert "Ask follow-up questions" in system_msg["content"]
   315→
   316→    @pytest.mark.asyncio
   317→    async def test_flattens_list_content(self):
   318→        """Messages with list-type content are flattened to text."""
   319→        runner = _make_runner()
   320→        runner._autoreply_configs["key"] = {
   321→            "prompt": "Continue", "model": None, "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, "turn_count": 0,
   322→        }
   323→        runner.session_store.load_transcript.return_value = [
   324→            {"role": "user", "content": [
   325→                {"type": "text", "text": "Look at this image"},
   326→                {"type": "image_url", "image_url": {"url": "http://example.com/img.png"}},
   327→            ]},
   328→            {"role": "assistant", "content": "I see the image."},
   329→        ]
   330→
   331→        mock_response = MagicMock()
   332→        mock_response.choices = [MagicMock()]
   333→        mock_response.choices[0].message.content = "What else can you tell me?"
   334→
   335→        with patch("agent.auxiliary_client.async_call_llm", return_value=mock_response) as mock_llm:
   336→            await runner._generate_autoreply("key", "sess_123")
   337→
   338→        # The user message should have been flattened to text only
   339→        messages = mock_llm.call_args[1]["messages"]
   340→        user_msg = [m for m in messages if m["role"] == "user" and "Look at" in m.get("content", "")]
   341→        assert len(user_msg) == 1
   342→        assert user_msg[0]["content"] == "Look at this image"
   343→
   344→    @pytest.mark.asyncio
   345→    async def test_uses_model_override(self):
   346→        runner = _make_runner()
   347→        runner._autoreply_configs["key"] = {
   348→            "prompt": "test", "model": "openai/gpt-4o-mini",
   349→            "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, "turn_count": 0,
   350→        }
   351→        runner.session_store.load_transcript.return_value = [
   352→            {"role": "user", "content": "hi"},
   353→            {"role": "assistant", "content": "hello"},
   354→        ]
   355→
   356→        mock_response = MagicMock()
   357→        mock_response.choices = [MagicMock()]
   358→        mock_response.choices[0].message.content = "reply"
   359→
   360→        with patch("agent.auxiliary_client.async_call_llm", return_value=mock_response) as mock_llm:
   361→            await runner._generate_autoreply("key", "sess_123")
   362→
   363→        assert mock_llm.call_args[1]["model"] == "openai/gpt-4o-mini"

   507→    @pytest.mark.asyncio

   584→class TestAutoreplyInHandleMessage:
   585→    """Tests for auto-reply wiring in _handle_message.
   586→
   587→    These test the auto-reply logic directly rather than going through the
   588→    full _handle_message pipeline, which requires too much setup. The
   589→    individual methods (_generate_autoreply, _process_autoreply) are
   590→    thoroughly tested above; here we verify the wiring code itself.
   591→    """
   592→
   593→    def test_autoreply_message_detected_by_message_id(self):
   594→        """Messages with autoreply- prefix in message_id are detected."""
   595→        event = _make_event(text="test", message_id="autoreply-123.456")
   596→        assert (event.message_id or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   597→
   598→        event2 = _make_event(text="test", message_id="regular-123")
   599→        assert not (event2.message_id or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   600→
   601→    def test_turn_counter_reset_logic(self):
   602→        """Real messages reset counter; autoreply messages don't."""
   603→        runner = _make_runner()
   604→        session_key = "agent:main:telegram:dm:12345"
   605→        runner._autoreply_configs[session_key] = {
   606→            "prompt": "test", "model": None, "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, "turn_count": 7,
   607→        }
   608→
   609→        # Real message — should reset
   610→        is_autoreply = ("regular-msg" or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   611→        if session_key in runner._autoreply_configs and not is_autoreply:
   612→            runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 0
   613→        assert runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] == 0
   614→
   615→        # Restore and test autoreply message — should NOT reset
   616→        runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 7
   617→        is_autoreply = ("autoreply-123.456" or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   618→        if session_key in runner._autoreply_configs and not is_autoreply:
   619→            runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 0
   620→        assert runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] == 7
   621→
```

```
+    async def _generate_autoreply(self, session_key: str, session_id: str):
+        """Generate an auto-reply using the auxiliary LLM client.
+
+        Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).  reply_text is None when
+        auto-reply is inactive or the turn cap was just hit.
+        """
+        from agent.autoreply import check_and_advance, build_autoreply_messages
+
+        config = self._autoreply_configs.get(session_key)
+        if not config:
+            return None, False
+
+        text, cap_reached = check_and_advance(config)
+        if cap_reached:
+            del self._autoreply_configs[session_key]
+            return None, True
+        if text:
+            return text, False
+
+        # LLM-generated: build prompt from transcript and call async LLM
+        history = self.session_store.load_transcript(session_id)
+        messages = build_autoreply_messages(config, history)
+
+        from agent.auxiliary_client import async_call_llm
+        call_kwargs = {
+            "task": "autoreply",
+            "messages": messages,
+            "temperature": 0.7,
+            "max_tokens": 1024,
+        }
+        if config.get("model"):
+            call_kwargs["model"] = config["model"]
+
+        response = await async_call_llm(**call_kwargs)
+        reply_text = response.choices[0].message.content.strip()
+        config["turn_count"] += 1
+        return reply_text, False
+
+    async def _process_autoreply(self, source, session_key: str, session_id: str):
+        """Fire-and-forget task: generate an auto-reply and inject it via the adapter."""
+        try:
+            adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
+            if not adapter:
+                return
+
+            autoreply_text, cap_reached = await self._generate_autoreply(
+                session_key, session_id)
+
+            if not autoreply_text:
+                if cap_reached:
+                    metadata = {"thread_id": source.thread_id} if source.thread_id else None
```

```
+def resolve_provider_credentials(
+    provider: str,
+    model: str,
+) -> Optional[dict]:
+    """Resolve a provider+model into a complete credentials bundle.
+
+    Thin wrapper around :func:`resolve_provider_client` that also derives
+    ``api_mode`` from the provider name.  Used by every code path that
+    needs to construct or switch to a new provider: fallback activation,
+    the ``switch_model`` tool, and per-task delegation overrides.
+
+    Returns a dict on success::
+
+        {
+            "provider": "openrouter",
+            "model": "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6",
+            "base_url": "https://...",
+            "api_key": "sk-...",
+            "api_mode": "chat_completions",
+            "client": <OpenAI>,       # ready-to-use client object
+        }
+
+    Returns ``None`` if the provider is not configured (no API key).
+    """
+    client, _ = resolve_provider_client(provider, model=model, raw_codex=True)
+    if client is None:
+        return None
+
+    api_mode = "chat_completions"
+    if provider == "openai-codex":
+        api_mode = "codex_responses"
+    elif provider == "anthropic":
+        api_mode = "anthropic_messages"
+
+    return {
+        "provider": provider,
+        "model": model,
+        "base_url": str(client.base_url),
+        "api_key": client.api_key,
+        "api_mode": api_mode,
+        "client": client,
+    }
+
+
```

```
+        """Start the control API in a background thread for external model switching."""

+    def _handle_autoreply_command(self, cmd: str):
+        """Handle /autoreply — enable, disable, or show auto-reply status."""
+        from agent.autoreply import parse_autoreply_args, format_status
+
+        args = cmd.split(None, 1)[1].strip() if len(cmd.split(None, 1)) > 1 else ""
+        action, new_config = parse_autoreply_args(args)
+
+        if action == "off":
+            if self._autoreply_config:
+                self._autoreply_config = None
+                _cprint("🔇 Auto-reply disabled.")
+            else:
+                _cprint("Auto-reply is not active.")
+        elif action.startswith("max:"):
+            n = int(action.split(":")[1])
+            if self._autoreply_config:
+                self._autoreply_config["max_turns"] = n
+                _cprint(f"🔄 Auto-reply max turns set to {n}.")
+            else:
+                _cprint("Auto-reply is not active. Use /autoreply <instructions> first.")
+        elif action.startswith("error:"):
+            _cprint(action[6:])
+        elif action == "status":
+            if self._autoreply_config:
+                _cprint("🔄 " + format_status(self._autoreply_config))
+            else:
+                _cprint("Auto-reply is not active. Use /autoreply <instructions> to enable.")
+        elif action == "enabled":
+            self._autoreply_config = new_config
+            mode = "literal mode " if new_config.get("literal") else ""
+            label = "Message" if new_config.get("literal") else "Prompt"
+            prompt_preview = new_config["prompt"][:80]
+            if len(new_config["prompt"]) > 80:
+                prompt_preview += "..."
+            limit = "forever" if new_config["max_turns"] == 0 else f"max {new_config['max_turns']} turns"
+            _cprint(f"🔄 Auto-reply enabled — {mode}({limit}).")
+            _cprint(f"  {label}: {prompt_preview}")
+            _cprint("  Send a message to start the loop. Use /autoreply off to stop.")
+
+    def _generate_autoreply_text(self) -> Optional[str]:
+        """Generate the next auto-reply text, or None if done."""
+        from agent.autoreply import check_and_advance, build_autoreply_messages
+
+        config = self._autoreply_config
+        if not config:
+            return None
+
+        text, cap_reached = check_and_advance(config)
+        if cap_reached:
+            self._autoreply_config = None
+            _cprint(f"\n{_DIM}[Auto-reply limit reached. Use /autoreply to re-enable.]{_RST}")
+            return None
+        if text:
+            return text
+
+        # LLM-generated: build prompt from conversation history and call sync LLM
+        from agent.auxiliary_client import call_llm
+
+        messages = build_autoreply_messages(config, self.conversation_history or [])
+        call_kwargs = {
+            "task": "autoreply",
+            "messages": messages,
+            "temperature": 0.7,
+            "max_tokens": 1024,
+        }
+        if config.get("model"):
+            call_kwargs["model"] = config["model"]
+
+        response = call_llm(**call_kwargs)
+        reply_text = response.choices[0].message.content.strip()
+        config["turn_count"] += 1
+        return reply_text
+
```

The auxiliary_client changes are for thread safety. Let me now create a comprehensive summary based on all my findings:

Based on my thorough trace of the Control API feature on the `poms-refactoring` branch, here's the complete dependency map:

## CORE CONTROL API FILES (must have)

1. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/control_api.py`**
   - The HTTP server itself (aiohttp-based)
   - Imports: aiohttp, json, logging, os
   - Provides: ControlAPI class, endpoints for health/sessions/message/commands
   - Key methods: `__init__`, `_setup_routes`, `health`, `list_sessions`, `get_session`, `list_commands`, `send_message`, `start`, `stop`

2. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/hermes_control_client.py`**
   - Lightweight stdlib-only client for external tools
   - Imports: json, os, urllib (only)
   - Provides: HermesControl class for discovering and calling the API
   - No external dependencies beyond Python stdlib

## GATEWAY INTEGRATION FILES

3. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py`**
   - **New methods added:**
     - `get_session_info(key)` - returns session state including autoreply config
     - `inject_message(key, text, interrupt)` - core method called by ControlAPI for message injection
     - `_handle_autoreply_command(event)` - slash command handler
     - `_generate_autoreply(session_key, session_id)` - generates auto-reply text via LLM
     - `_process_autoreply(source, session_key, session_id)` - fire-and-forget task for auto-reply generation/injection
   - **New state:** `_autoreply_configs` dict per session
   - **New imports:** `import time` (for message IDs)
   - **Modified imports:** Removed `DeliveryTarget` from gateway.delivery import
   - **Control API startup:** Lines ~4005-4037 check HERMES_CONTROL_API env var and start ControlAPI
   - **Modified _handle_message:** Added autoreply message detection, counter reset, response labeling
   - **Modified /stop command:** Cleans up autoreply config, adds notification about disable
   - **Modified /reset command:** Cleans up autoreply config
   - **Modified _known_commands:** Added "autoreply" to the set

4. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/platforms/base.py`**
   - **Modified `handle_message(event, *, interrupt=True)` signature**
   - Now accepts `interrupt` kwarg (default True) to queue vs interrupt
   - Stores message in pending dict but only signals interrupt if `interrupt=True`
   - Different print messages for interrupt vs queue modes
   - No new imports

5. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/__init__.py`**
   - **Minor change:** Moved SessionResetPolicy import from session to config module
   - No Control API specific changes

## NEW FILES - AUTO-REPLY ENGINE

6. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/agent/autoreply.py`** (NEW)
   - Shared auto-reply logic for CLI and gateway
   - Key functions:
     - `parse_autoreply_args(args)` - parses /autoreply command
     - `build_autoreply_messages(config, history)` - builds LLM prompt
     - `check_and_advance(config)` - handles turn cap and literal mode
     - `format_status(config)` - formats status display
   - No external dependencies

## CLI INTEGRATION FILES

7. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py`**
   - **New method `_start_control_api()`** - starts ControlAPI in background thread for CLI
   - Creates a shim class `_CLIRunner` that mimics GatewayRunner._running_agents interface
   - Implements `inject_message()` to put text in CLI's `_pending_input` or `_interrupt_queue`
   - Implements `get_session_info()` to return autoreply config state
   - **New method `_handle_autoreply_command(cmd)`** - CLI version of autoreply handler
   - **New method `_generate_autoreply_text()`** - generates next auto-reply (sync version using call_llm not async_call_llm)
   - **New state:** `_autoreply_config` dict (per CLI session)
   - **In chat loop:** Resets turn counter on real messages, auto-detects [autoreply] prefix, generates/injects replies
   - **In chat loop:** Labels responses with turn count like `[Auto-reply N/MAX]`
   - **Imports added:** asyncio, threading, time (for API startup)
   - **Modified banner imports:** now calls functions `_build_hermes_logo()`, `_build_hermes_caduceus()`, `_build_compact_banner()` instead of using static strings

8. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/agent/auxiliary_client.py`**
   - **New function `resolve_provider_credentials(provider, model)`** - wraps resolve_provider_client with api_mode derivation
   - **Threading safety:** Added `_client_cache_lock` (threading.Lock) protecting `_client_cache` access for concurrent subagent threads
   - **Async LLM client:** Already had `async_call_llm()` function (not new, but heavily used by _generate_autoreply)
   - Minor cleanup: removed unused temperature variable in _CodexCompletionsAdapter

## CONFIGURATION FILES

9. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/.env.example`**
   - **Added section for Control API:**
     - HERMES_CONTROL_API=true (opt-in, default off)
     - HERMES_CONTROL_PORT=47823 (override default)

## TEST FILES (reference only, not required for feature)

- **`tests/gateway/test_control_api.py`** - tests for ControlAPI HTTP endpoints
- **`tests/gateway/test_autoreply_command.py`** - comprehensive autoreply tests

## CRITICAL IMPORTS CHAIN

For Control API to work standalone on the gateway:

```
gateway/control_api.py
  └─ imports: aiohttp.web (async HTTP)
     
gateway/run.py 
  ├─ imports: gateway.control_api.ControlAPI
  ├─ imports: agent.autoreply (for _handle_autoreply_command)
  ├─ imports: agent.auxiliary_client.async_call_llm (for _generate_autoreply)
  ├─ imports: time (for autoreply message IDs)
  └─ new state: _autoreply_configs, _running_agents, inject_message method

gateway/platforms/base.py
  └─ modified: handle_message(event, interrupt=True) signature

agent/autoreply.py (NEW)
  ├─ parse_autoreply_args()
  ├─ build_autoreply_messages()
  ├─ check_and_advance()
  └─ format_status()
```

## FOR CLI MODE:

```
cli.py
  ├─ imports: gateway.control_api.ControlAPI
  ├─ imports: agent.autoreply (for _handle_autoreply_command)
  ├─ imports: agent.auxiliary_client.call_llm (for _generate_autoreply_text)
  ├─ imports: asyncio, threading, time
  └─ new state: _autoreply_config (per session)
```

## KEY FEATURES THAT DEPEND ON CONTROL API

1. **Message Injection** - inject_message() in GatewayRunner and CLI shim
2. **Session Querying** - list sessions, get session info, resolve "_any"
3. **Auto-reply Loop** - /autoreply command enables autonomous message generation
4. **Interrupt vs Queue Modes** - control message injection timing via interrupt parameter
5. **Port File Discovery** - ~/.hermes/control_api.port written by start(), read by HermesControl client

All changes are fully backward compatible (Control API disabled by default via HERMES_CONTROL_API env var).
